# `#521` mk48nvramkill -- PASS 2 on the real diff

Nonce: `PASS2_MK48_20260910`. Open with `VERDICT:` (RATIFY / NOT-RATIFY / BREACH-FOUND), then echo the
nonce.

Ordinary correctness review of a device model in an emulator written in C99. **The tree is
`C:\DocumentNoSnc\CC\GXEMUL\GXEMUL-SEC\`**; three unrelated 2021-vintage copies sit elsewhere under
the project root. If you cannot open files, answer from this packet alone and SAY SO -- the packet is
self-contained and names any premise you cannot settle from it.

**Please do not simply ratify.** Three briefs of mine this week had premises refuted by a seat that opened
a file, and each refutation improved the round. **Check the premises, not just the derivations.**

## THE DEFECT (witnessed at rung 3 BEFORE the edit)

`dev_mk48txx.c` registers the MK48T08 for `MK48TXX_LEN` = `MK48T08_CLKSZ` = **8192** bytes. An accept
block passed everything OUTSIDE `[MK48T08_CLKOFF, MK48T08_CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ISEC)` to the RAM path via
`goto ret`. With `CLKOFF` = `0x1ff0` and `ISEC` = `9` the switch was reached for `0x1ff0..0x1ff8`, and it
holds exactly ONE case, `CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ICSR` = `0x1ff8`. **The other eight offsets fell to `default:`
and called `exit(1)`.** `machine_mvme88k.c` attaches the device unconditionally in its MVME187 arm.

**MEASURED, not argued:** a rung-3 witness on unmodified `-E mvme88k -e mvme187` scored **15/47** on the
committed build, all 15 controls green and all 32 kill rows red. **The killing population is SIXTEEN
cells** (eight offsets x read and write), and **the kill is WIDTH-INDEPENDENT** -- `len` never enters the
guard, so `ld`, `ld.h` and `ld.bu` all die at the same start offset.

**Those eight are ORDINARY NVRAM on this part**, which is what licenses accepting them. `mk48txxreg.h`
says the first bank of eight registers at `(nvramsz - 16)` -- exactly `0x1ff0` -- is *"available only on
more recent MK48Txx models"*, naming them IFLAGS, IASEC, IAMIN, IAHOUR, IADAY, IINTR, IWDOG. They are the
MK48T59's alarm and watchdog bank; this file implements the MK48T08 alone. Storage already existed
(`reg` is `MK48TXX_LEN` bytes).

## THE DETECTOR AND ITS KILL TABLE

Shipped detector `regress/mk48_nvram_probe.py`, 48 rows. It is the round's witness with ONE row inverted:
the witness asserted 16 offset-named death messages -- green only while the defect LIVES -- and the
detector asserts ZERO instead.

Table 2, `regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/t2/SCORES.txt`, scratch clone, one frozen
instrument for every leg, restore verified on both parts:

```
=== SUMMARY ===
BASE      MK48_RESULT=48/48      
M_OLD     MK48_RESULT=15/48      rd 1ff0,rd 1ff0,rd 1ff1,rd 1ff1,rd 1ff2,rd 1ff2,rd 1ff3,rd 1ff3,rd 1ff4,rd 1ff4,rd 1ff5,rd 1ff5,rd 1ff6,rd 1ff6,rd 1ff7,rd 1ff7,wr 1ff0,wr 1ff0,wr 1ff1,wr 1ff1,wr 1ff2,wr 1ff2,wr 1ff3,wr 1ff3,wr 1ff4,wr 1ff4,wr 1ff5,wr 1ff5,wr 1ff6,wr 1ff6,wr 1ff7,wr 1ff7,msg silent
M_HALF    MK48_RESULT=31/48      rd 1ff4,rd 1ff4,rd 1ff5,rd 1ff5,rd 1ff6,rd 1ff6,rd 1ff7,rd 1ff7,wr 1ff4,wr 1ff4,wr 1ff5,wr 1ff5,wr 1ff6,wr 1ff6,wr 1ff7,wr 1ff7,msg silent
M_ICSR    MK48_RESULT=47/48      csr rise
M_NULL    MK48_RESULT=22/48      live absent,nvram0 absent,devsig yr,devsig mon,devsig mday,devsig wday,ok rd,ok rd,csr rise,rd 1ff0,rd 1ff0,rd 1ff1,rd 1ff1,rd 1ff2,rd 1ff2,rd 1ff3,rd 1ff3,rd 1ff4,rd 1ff4,rd 1ff5,rd 1ff5,rd 1ff6,rd 1ff6,rd 1ff7,rd 1ff7,msg silent
HEAD      MK48_RESULT=15/48      rd 1ff0,rd 1ff0,rd 1ff1,rd 1ff1,rd 1ff2,rd 1ff2,rd 1ff3,rd 1ff3,rd 1ff4,rd 1ff4,rd 1ff5,rd 1ff5,rd 1ff6,rd 1ff6,rd 1ff7,rd 1ff7,wr 1ff0,wr 1ff0,wr 1ff1,wr 1ff1,wr 1ff2,wr 1ff2,wr 1ff3,wr 1ff3,wr 1ff4,wr 1ff4,wr 1ff5,wr 1ff5,wr 1ff6,wr 1ff6,wr 1ff7,wr 1ff7,msg silent
RESTORED  MK48_RESULT=48/48      
KILLTABLE_DONE
```

**TABLE 1 FOUND A FLOOR BREACH AND IT WAS REPAIRED IN-ROUND.** `M_ICSR` -- moving the accept block's
UPPER bound to `CLKOFF + ICSR` -- escaped **47/47**. The mutated condition
`addr < 0x1ff8 || addr >= 0x1ff8` is a **tautology**, so the switch is never reached and `0x1ff8` is
served as plain RAM; the control register silently stops working. The rows aimed at that offset could not
see it because they are **survive-only** there: they assert the access ran to completion, which is true on
the RAM path too. The repair is one row, `csr rise`, reading the case's SIDE EFFECT -- writing the READ
bit 0 to 1 calls `mk48txx_update_regs()`, so the row clears the control register, poisons a clock slot,
drives the edge and reads the slot back.

## THE QUESTIONS

1. **IS THE FIX RIGHT?** Is `CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ICSR` the correct lower bound, or should it be something
   else? Does the change swallow, or otherwise disturb, the one case the switch legitimately implements?
2. **IS THE UPPER BOUND STILL RIGHT?** It is deliberately unchanged at `MK48TXX_ISEC`, which routes the
   time-of-day registers to the RAM path. Is that correct, harmless, or a separate defect?
3. **A FLOOR PROBLEM I WANT SCRUTINISED RATHER THAN WAVED THROUGH.** This diff **removes no kill
   statement** -- it makes the existing `default:` arm UNREACHABLE. So floor clause (b), a verbatim
   reinstatement of the removed defect, cannot be satisfied literally: reinstating that arm with the
   corrected boundary is a no-op or dead code and no channel can distinguish it. I substituted `M_OLD`,
   restoring the old dispatch. **Is that substitution legitimate, or is the round claiming a floor it does
   not meet?**
4. **WHAT MUTANT ESCAPES THIS 48-ROW INSTRUMENT?** The table found one breach already and I would rather
   find the second here than after shipping. Name a concrete edit and say which row you predict it
   reddens, or say plainly that you predict none within the enumerated class -- and if so, state the class
   BOUNDARY, i.e. which edits are outside it and are therefore disclosed debt rather than rowed.
5. **IS ANY OF THE 48 ROWS VACUOUS?** A row whose named channel cannot vary under its named mutant is
   vacuous by construction. I already know `ok rd/wr 1ff8` were vacuous against `M_ICSR` and that is what
   the new row repairs. **Is any OTHER row in the same position?**
6. **A SECOND, NON-FATAL DEFECT sits three lines above and is deliberately OUT OF SCOPE**: the accept path
   reads and writes exactly ONE byte whatever `len` says, so a four-byte read of the clock block returns a
   single zero-extended byte. Is leaving it out of this round right, or does routing eight more offsets to
   that path make it this round's business?
7. Anything else.

Read-only: no builds, no probe runs, no edits.


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

R1 WAS NOT, and the comment left on the TS arm CLAIMED IT WAS COVERED: 'a break here falls through to the RS switch, which for most values complains too' -- true of most values, and R1 was using the one that does not. A WRONG RECORD, in the same file, in the same session. THE KEEPER: FIXING TWO OF THREE SIBLINGS AND WRITING A COMMENT ASSERTING THE THIRD WAS COVERED IS HOW A WRONG RECORD SHIPS. *** THE RS VARIANT IS THE SERIOUS ONE BECAUSE IT PUTS exit(1) BACK. ***  With `return` at the RS default the function leaves before `if (cause_interrupt)`. Replace it with `break` and that site becomes reachable from ALL SIXTEEN resource-select encodings instead of the one X1 pins -- host death this diff removed, restored by one keyword. No reject row could see it, because every one had IE clear. MEASURED after the seat said plainly 'not recompiled here': TS `break` 16/16 SURVIVED, RS `break` 16/16 SURVIVED, and its third remark -- `fatal`->`debug` as 'one identifier and also 16/16' -- measured true as well, invisible to every row because they all single-step, where debug() prints exactly like fatal(). That is not coverage debt. Pre-fix each of those exited. The round's own comment claimed the opposite. Closed by returning before BOTH tails on any unaligned access, whose soundness is an INJECTIVITY PROOF -- on multiples of 4 the map off -> off/4 is injective, so an aligned offset reaching the arm cannot share an index with any implemented case, because if it did it would BE that case -- and a seat re-derived that proof independently before it was believed. opus5 then built FOUR mutants that scored a full 27/27 against the detector: `& 3` -> `& 1` (a 16-bit store at +0x06 is 2-byte aligned, architecturally legal, and re-opens the alias); the guard restricted to MEM_WRITE (the read half the comment claimed and no row pinned); and each arm keyed into the OTHER window's latch array, one of which additionally writes past the shorter row onto dma_reg[0] -- invisible to ASan, being member-to-member inside one malloc. *** `dma alias`, THE ROW WRITTEN TO PIN THAT VERY REPAIR, WAS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO THE FIRST *** because it uses an ODD address that both guard forms drop: a row written from the fix's intent tests the intent, not the boundary. For the two\ninterrupt stubs none of them applies: *\"declining an interrupt leaves the machine incomplete.\"*  A stub is not a\nrefusal to service a value; it is an unimplemented PATH, and the disposition is to write the code.  This is the\noption-set completeness rule paying off exactly as it is meant to -- the member was found by a seat reading the\ntree, not by argument.\n\n*** AND THE FAULT MEMBER IS NOT AVAILABLE ON THIS MACHINE AT ALL. ***  `dev_unreadable.c` works because\n`memory_rw.c` dispatches an exception on a failed device access **for MIPS and M88K, with NO ALPHA EQUIVALENT**.\nSo the idiom this fork installs ten times in `machine_sgi.c` does not transfer here, and any round reaching for\nFAULT owes Alpha exception delivery first.  *A precedent that is real elsewhere in the tree is not thereby\ntransferable; that is the warrant-transfer rule, and it took a seat opening `memory_rw.c` to see it.*\n\n**AND A FINDING ABOUT THE ALPHA PORT THAT OUTRANKS THIS ROW:** the Alpha interrupt callbacks merely set\n`irq_asserted`, and **the execution loop never consumes that flag**.  So implementing the two stubs would not by\nitself deliver an interrupt -- removing the exits cannot complete the path.  Filed as its own question rather\nthan folded in here.\n\n## THE PREMISE AUDIT, and I was wrong three times\n\n* **PREMISE 1 HOLDS.**  Twelve textual occurrences, eleven outside comments.\n* **PREMISE 2's REMOVAL IS REAL; ITS \"USABLE PRECEDENT\" CONCLUSION IS WRONG.**  I read the commented-out\n  `dev >= 21` kill plus its live `dev = 0` as the file's own answer, transferable to this round.  It is not: it\n  **ALIASES \"no selected device\" to DEVICE ZERO**, which is harmless today only because device 7 is the sole\n  installed device and slot 0 happens to be empty.  Populate slot 0 and it is a latent alias defect.  *So the\n  question I asked -- is this a sound precedent or a latent defect of its own -- has the second answer, and my\n  brief had already leaned on the first.*\n* **PREMISE 3's CLASS COUNTS HOLD, ITS SINGLETON CLAIM DOES NOT.**  `W_BASE0` accepts **TWO** values, `0` and\n  `0x300800000` (`idata != 0ULL && idata != 0x300800000ULL`), so \"four registers accepting exactly ONE value\" is\n  wrong -- only **three** window guards accept a single value.  *This one is mine twice over: the original row\n  note said it, I re-derived the enumeration tonight and reproduced it, and the guard's own text was in my\n  terminal when I did.*\n* **PREMISE 4 HOLDS** as one machine-setup consumer (AlphaBook 1), with no dedicated boot rig -- **but\n  \"never exercised\" DOES NOT FOLLOW**: a generic constructor sweep exists (`gate_asan_sweep.sh`), without\n  proving coverage of these branches.  My \"no rig means no coverage\" was too strong.\n* **PREMISE 5 IS AN UNSUPPORTED RESTRICTION.**  I said a guest here is firmware, so the reaching value is\n  whatever SRM or NetBSD writes.  Guest KERNEL stores use the same MMIO path, and interrupt callbacks arise from\n  DEVICE events -- neither is firmware.\n\n## DISPOSITIONS IT WOULD TAKE, per class\n* **INTERRUPT STUB (2):** IMPLEMENT forwarding and CPU interrupt delivery.\n* **BUS (1):** implement Type-1 decoding then ACCEPT through `bus_pci`, using its existing absent-device\n  handling.  `ioc_conf & 1` selects the CONFIGURATION-CYCLE TYPE and is **not the decoded bus number** -- and\n  deleting this exit alone leaves `dev` uninitialised.\n* **RANGE (1):** DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE with initialised read results and no forwarded side effects.  Note\n  `ofs` is at most three, **so this guard cannot fire for four- or eight-byte accesses** -- changing `>`\n  mechanically is not a design.\n* **VALUE (6):** ACCEPT documented register fields using the existing backing, latched announce for the missing\n  DMA/TLB effects.  **Every guarded assignment already happens BEFORE its check**, so no invented backing is\n  needed -- but readback alone does not implement translation.\n* **CATCH-ALL (1):** split documented registers (`STAT0`/`STAT1`) from genuine holes; FAULT only for confirmed\n  holes and only once Alpha exception delivery exists.\n\n**WHAT IT COULD NOT ESTABLISH, said plainly:** source shows connected interrupt paths and conditional MMIO paths\nfor the other nine sites, but **no particular SRM/NetBSD execution of any terminating site**.  Firmware\nreachability and failure frequency remain unmeasured, so the severity order is provisional on exactly that.\n\n\n*** THE SEVERITY WARRANT THIS ROW WAS MISSING: THE REJECTED VALUES INCLUDE A DOCUMENTED ONE, AND THE\nSTORE LANDS BEFORE THE PROCESS ENDS.  Found by the codex seat 2026-09-07 (regress/artifacts/lcabadval/codex_scoping_20260907.txt),\nverified against the source and the header before recording. ***\n\nEach of the three window guards has this shape:\n\n    d->window_mask_0 = idata;                          <- the store LANDS\n    if (idata != 0x700000ULL) { fatal(...); exit(1); } <- then the process ends\n\n**And zero is a documented encoding.**  From src/include/thirdparty/alpha_lcareg.h:\n\n    #define IOC_W_MASK_1M   0x0000000000000000ULL   /* 1MB window */\n\nSo a guest selecting the SMALLEST DOCUMENTED WINDOW SIZE ends the emulator.  The same argument covers\nW_BASE1, whose one accepted constant 0x240000000 carries IOC_W_BASE_WEN (0x200000000, *Window enable*) --\n**so a driver DISABLING a window, by writing a base with WEN clear, is killed too.**\n\n*** THIS IS #504's WARRANT AT A SECOND DEVICE: documented values that the model kills on. ***  It is not a\njunk-value guard; it is a whitelist of ONE that excludes documented members of its own encoding.  codex adds\nthat all three registers start at zero through constructor initialisation, so the rejected value is also the\nRESET value.\n\n**AND THE RANGE SITE IS FAR MORE REACHABLE THAN THIS ROW SAID.**  It was recorded as `ofs > len'.  Reading\nDEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa): `relative_addr >>= 5; ofs = relative_addr & 3;' -- **ofs is guest ADDRESS BITS 5-6**,\nnot a written value.  So an ordinary 1-byte access at an address with those bits set to 2 or 3 ends the\nprocess.  That is address decoding, not a value whitelist, and it belongs in a different disposition class\nfrom the six value guards.  The rest of that function is filed separately as [[lcaisalane]].\n\n*** DISPOSITION PASS, codex 2026-09-07 (regress/artifacts/lcabadval/codex_disposition_20260907.txt).  IT NARROWS MY REACHABILITY CLAIM,\nCONFIRMS TWO OTHERS, AND SUPPLIES THE IN-TREE PRECEDENTS THE OPTION-SET RULE ASKS FOR -- INCLUDING\nCONFLICTING ONES, WHICH IS WHAT I ASKED IT TO SURFACE. ***\n\nVerdict: *\"Disposition 5 is unavailable through Alpha's failed-device-access path.  Five candidate rounds fit\na uniform VALUE-refusal policy.  The tree supplies CONFLICTING precedents, especially for lane handling and\nstoring unsupported values.\"*\n\n*** MY `AN ORDINARY READ-MODIFY-WRITE ENDS THE EMULATOR' CLAIM IS TOO BROAD, AND I HAVE REPEATED IT IN THREE\nPLACES. ***  codex: *\"they read zero INITIALLY, then echo accepted writes.  Writing back the initial zero\nkills the last three; an arbitrary RMW does not necessarily kill them -- its resulting value matters.\"*\n\n**The precise claim is the INITIAL-ZERO readback**: before any accepted write, `W_MASK0', `W_BASE1' and\n`W_MASK1' read 0 and accept only one non-zero constant each, so a read-modify-write performed on a\nfreshly-reset device writes 0 back and ends the process.  **Once a valid value has been written the register\nechoes it**, and an RMW of that value is accepted.  So the window is early -- driver probe or\ninitialisation -- rather than any RMW at any time.  That is still a routine thing for a driver to do at\nattach, and it is still reached with a documented encoding (`IOC_W_MASK_1M` = 0), but the sentence I wrote\nwas wider than the fact.\n\n**CONFIRMED, both of which I had asserted:** every VALUE arm STORES BEFORE CHECKING; and the accepted sets\nare exactly as filed (`IOC_CONF' {0,1}, `IOC_TB_ENA' {0,0x80}, `W_BASE0' {0,0x300800000}, and the three\nsingle-value ones).\n\n**DISPOSITION 5 IS UNAVAILABLE -- now established from a third direction.**  The carrier already recorded it\ngenerally, [[alphafaultclaim]] reached it from the instruction side, and this reaches it from the\nfailed-device-access path.  **Any proposal for a bus-error refusal on an Alpha device owes Alpha exception\ndelivery first**, which is a far larger round than the one it would be riding on.\n\n**THE PRECEDENT I ASKED FOR, AND IT IS AN EXACT ONE:** `cpu_sh.c' defines `sh3_cpu_interrupt_assert()' and\n`sh3_cpu_interrupt_deassert()' with EMPTY `/* TODO */' bodies -- *\"an exact unimplemented-callback precedent\nfor 2, ignore silently\"* -- and `core/interrupt.c' defines empty `no_interrupt_assert/deassert' callbacks,\nthough those represent intentionally absent connections rather than unimplemented ones.  **So the two\nINTERRUPT STUBS have a direct in-tree answer** and do not need disposition 7 after all, which is what I had\nassumed they needed.\n\n**AND IT REPORTS THE PRECEDENTS CONFLICT** -- especially on lane handling and on whether to store an\nunsupported value. And a correction to my own brief: only TWO BITS IN STAT0 are R/W1C, not STAT1.\n\n*** TITLE CORRECTED 2026-09-07: it said FOUR registers and the true count is SIX. *** Derived, not recalled: `exit(1)` occurs SEVEN times in DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc) -- six guarded value-arms (LCA_IOC_CONF :215, TB_ENA :235, W_BASE0 :250, W_MASK0 :265, W_BASE1 :289, W_MASK1 :304) and the `default:` arm at :324, which this round does NOT close.  The four window registers are presumably what the row was opened on; CONF and TB_ENA carry the same shape and were folded in later without the title following.  **A stale title is how a queue row is READ** -- the brief that goes to the panel says six, and a reader comparing the two would have had to guess which was right.  W_T_BASE0 (:272) and W_T_BASE1 (:311) confirmed UNGUARDED: plain readback/store, no value test -- which is the sibling warrant the design brief puts to the panel.\n\n*** AND THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE WAS ITSELF CORRUPTED WHEN FIRST WRITTEN -- BY THE SHELL, QUIETLY\nENOUGH TO SURVIVE REVIEW. ***  It was applied through `python -c \"...\"` with DOUBLE quotes, and\nbash performed COMMAND SUBSTITUTION on the backticks inside it: `exit(1)` and `default:` were\nEXECUTED and replaced by their empty output, leaving readable prose with two words silently\nmissing -- \"Derived, not recalled:  occurs SEVEN times\".  bash did print two errors, but python\nran on the mangled source anyway and the write SUCCEEDED, so the record was wrong while the\ncommand reported success.\n\n**Inside a double-quoted shell argument a backtick is a COMMAND, not a quote mark.**  That is the\ncarrier's backslash-through-heredoc class arriving on a new character, and with the same\nsignature: the failure lands in the FILE, not in the exit status.\n\n*** AND THE REPAIR FAILED TWICE MORE BEFORE IT LANDED, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING. ***  The\nfix was retried as `python -c '...'` with SINGLE quotes -- correct for backticks -- and bash\nrejected it, because the note explaining the hazard contains the word \"carrier's\", whose\nAPOSTROPHE terminated the shell quote.  So: double quotes lose to backticks, single quotes lose to\napostrophes, and English prose about shell quoting reliably contains both.\n\n**The rule is not a better quoting style.  It is: PROSE DESTINED FOR A RECORD GOES INTO A SCRIPT\nFILE, never through `-c`.**  This paragraph was written that way and landed intact on the first\nattempt.\n\n*** PASS-1 DESIGN PANEL 2026-09-07 (preserved: regress/artifacts/lcabadval/panel_p1_20260907/ -- _scratchpad is never committed). Any ruling\nshort of the roster is provisional on its face, so no disposition is adopted below. ***\n\nSEATS: grok, glm, deepseek, minimax and the kimi-k3:cloud SUBSTITUTE answered (5); codex IN FLIGHT;\nthe kimi CLI WALLED on an explicit 403 weekly limit, so that seat is COVERED, not lost; agy\nDELIBERATELY NOT FIRED (measurement reserve, owner ruling 2026-08-30); opus5 and fable5 NOT FIRED,\nreserved for the 2026-09-09 adjudication.\n\n*** THE PANEL'S DOMINANT FINDING IS THAT MY BRIEF POSED THE WRONG QUESTION, AND FOUR SEATS REACHED\nIT INDEPENDENTLY: THE FORK IS MIS-POSED AS ONE DISPOSITION FOR ALL SIX ARMS. ***\n\nThe kimi substitute put it sharpest, and it is a self-inflicted wound: *\"The fork as posed (one\nanswer for the file) repeats the exact error the #447 census inlines a warning against.\"*  **My\nbrief QUOTED `#447`'s own sentence -- \"Saying 'the store is upstream' once for the file would have\nbeen wrong three times out of four. For the five,\npremise 6 survives -- grok's formulation is the one to keep: **\"B copies RCR1's *edit* without\nRCR1's *reason*.\"**  RCR1's warrant was that the stored value has a meaning the model otherwise\nhonours; for the five window/TB_ENA fields nothing honours anything, so the edit does not transfer.\nThe kimi substitute sharpens even the agreement: the right analogy for the five is not \"the lie is\npre-existing\" but **`#447`'s ICR cell -- *no honored state to corrupt*** -- which is a stronger and\ndifferently-grounded warrant than the one my premise 6 offered.\n\n**WHERE THEY SPLIT, AND IT IS A REAL DISPUTE TO SETTLE BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN BY COUNT:**\n\n    CONF -> B                 glm       \"premise 2 names a live consumer, so CONF is exactly RCR1-shaped\"\n    CONF -> A, more so        minimax   \"the consumer uses only bit 0, so a rejected value leaves it\n                                         correct -- and B can SILENTLY BREAK it if the retained old\n                                         value differs in bit 0\"\n    CONF -> decline or mask   kimi      split per arm\n    CONF -> neither           grok      \"treat it as a bit-mask leftover, not a window\"\n\n**minimax's objection to B is the one with a measurable consequence** and it is the opposite of\nglm's: under B a rejected CONF write leaves the PREVIOUS value in place, and if the previous value\ndiffers in bit 0 the PCI bus selection silently changes -- so B is not merely conservative there, it\ncan alter a consumer that A leaves correct.  That is checkable and should be checked before either\nis adopted.\n\n**THE DISSENT, recorded as a dissent and not as noise:** deepseek alone says premise 6 is unsound\nfor all six -- *\"the guard's accepted-value set is the model's CONTRACT.  Storing a rejected value\nand returning it on readback creates a new false belief that the value is supported, even if\naccepted values are also unimplemented.  The existing lie for accepted values does not license\nexpanding the set of lies.\"*  It also warns the reasoning generalises: it would justify removing any\nstore-before-guard exit anywhere.  **That generalisation objection is the strongest single argument\nagainst A and is not answered by the five-versus-one split**, because it attacks the warrant rather\nthan its scope.\n\n**A BRIEF DEFECT OF MINE, named by glm and correct:** premise 2's field counts were *asserted, not\ninlined* -- I ran the greps and wrote the conclusion instead of the OUTPUT.  This project's own rule\nis that a brief inlines the DERIVER'S OUTPUT rather than a literal, precisely so a packet-fed seat\ncan check it.  glm could not settle five of the six arms from the packet for exactly that reason and\nsaid so honestly rather than ratifying.\n\n**NOT ADOPTED, PENDING codex AND THE ROSTER.**  The next brief is per-arm by construction, carries\nthe deriver output rather than its summary, and puts minimax's silently-broken-consumer prediction\nas a question a measurement can answer.\n\n*** THE grok BODY, READ IN FULL 2026-09-07 -- AND NONE OF THE FOLLOWING WAS IN ITS VERDICT LINE.\nThis is the read-the-body rule paying off on the same day a row was filed about not reading. ***\n\n**1. IT ANSWERS deepseek's DISSENT, which the five/one split could not.**  deepseek's objection was\nthat premise 6 generalises -- it would license removing any store-before-guard exit anywhere.  grok\nscopes it precisely: *\"premise 6 is not the reasoning that lets every store-before-guard site keep\nits store.  It is the reasoning that lets a site keep its store WHEN NO ACCEPTED VALUE OF THAT FIELD\nIS CONSUMED.  That is #447's own per-site rule, not an evasion of it.\"*  The warrant is a per-site\ntest with a checkable predicate, not a blanket.\n\n**2. PREMISE 5 SCORED: \"The comments exist.  The transfer does not.\"**  RCR1 refuses CONSUMED\nFEATURE BITS; these guards whitelist UNIMPLEMENTED CONSTANTS.  That is the distinction the whole\nfork turned on, and it is sharper than my premise 6 offered.\n\n**3. *** OPTION A OPENS A NEW KILL PATH, AND THIS IS THE ROUND'S SAFETY FINDING. ***  Under A, a CONF\nwrite with extra bits that ALSO sets bit 0 reaches `lca_pci_conf`'s `bus != 0` exit(1) -- *\"newly\nreachable ... today that write dies in the CONF guard first.\"*  **A is not purely a reduction; it\nconverts one kill into a different reachable one.**  Any round shipping A must say so, and should\nconsider whether `lca_pci_conf` is in scope with it.\n\n**4. THE FILE'S OWN IDIOM IS A, AND THE GUARDS ARE THE OUTLIERS.**  In the SAME switch:\n`LCA_IOC_BASE` (HAE) and `TBIA` already silently ignore; `W_T_BASE0/1` already accept-and-store; and\nthe `dev >= 21` loop already deletes a kill and substitutes a default.  *\"The value guards are the\noutliers in THIS switch, not the house style.\"*  That is a stronger warrant than anything my brief\noffered, and it is one grep away.\n\n**5. THE ANNOUNCE TEXT WOULD BECOME A WRONG RECORD THE MOMENT A LANDS.**  The current string says\n*\"the only implemented value\"* about a value that **is not implemented**.  The honest once-message is\n*\"window translation / extra CONF bits not implemented\"*, not *\"illegal encoding\"*.  Rewrite it or\ndelete it with the exit -- do not carry it forward.\n\n**6. OPTION-SET CORRECTIONS.**  `#443`'s restore is *\"B wearing extra lines\"* -- observationally B\nfor readback, since nothing re-enters between the store and the `break`; not a distinct member.\n**STAT0 is the architecturally correct error path for LCA** (`IOC_STAT0_ERR` is R/W1C) and is\n**BLOCKED**: STAT0/STAT1 have no case and hit `default:` exit(1) -- *\"You cannot report an error\nthrough a register that currently kills the host.\"*  That member owes a STAT0 implementation first,\nwhich is a different round.  **FAULT confirmed unavailable**: `memory_rw.c` raises on `res <= 0` only\nunder MEM_MIPS and MEM_M88K; Alpha compiles with MEM_ALPHA and has no arm, so `return 0` fails the\naccess with no guest exception.\n\n**7. Premise 3 stands and masking would be an addition without a\nwarrant.\n\n*** 8. THE WITNESS IS NOT PENDING -- THE RECIPE EXISTS. THE\nHEADLINE COUNT (eleven live terminating calls) IS RIGHT.  THE ENUMERATION UNDER IT IS WRONG IN TWO WAYS, AND THE\nFIRST ONE CHANGES THE FIX DESIGN. ***\n\n**1.  `dev >= 21` IS NOT A LIVE KILL.  IT IS ALREADY DE-FATALISED -- AND IT IS THIS FILE'S OWN FIX PRECEDENT.**\n\nThe row lists it among \"two range guards that also kill\".  The source says otherwise: the guard is at `:127`, its\nbody is a COMMENTED-OUT `fatal()` + `exit(1)` at `:128-129`, and what actually executes is `dev = 0;` at `:130`.\n\n```c\n\tif (dev >= 21) {\n\t\t/*  fatal(\"[ LCA: No bus 0 device? TODO ]\\n\");\n\t\texit(1);  */\n\t\tdev = 0;\n\t}\n```\n\nSomeone has already made, in this very file, the decision this row exists to make: **delete the kill, substitute a\nsafe default, keep going.**  That is a TRANSFERRED WARRANT from inside the same file, which the six-member\noption-set rule says to go looking for -- *\"grep the fork for an existing idiom; the tree's own precedents are part\nof the design space\"* -- and which nobody had found because the row was read rather than re-derived.  So only ONE\nrange guard is live: `ofs > len` at `:163`.\n\n**2.  THE ENUMERATION COVERED 8 OF THE 11, AND THE THREE IT MISSED ARE NOT VALUE GUARDS AT ALL.**\n\nRe-derived by `grep -n 'exit(1)' dev_lca.c` minus the commented one (12 matches, 11 live), the eleven are:\n\n| line | class | condition |\n|---|---|---|\n| 73  | **INTERRUPT STUB** | `lca_interrupt_assert` -- bare `fatal(\"...TODO\"); exit(1)`, **NO GUARD** |\n| 85  | **INTERRUPT STUB** | `lca_interrupt_deassert` -- likewise |\n| 134 | BUS | `bus != 0`, i.e. `d->ioc_conf & 1` |\n| 163 | RANGE | `ofs > len` |\n| 206 | VALUE | `idata & ~1` (IOC_CONF) |\n| 226 | VALUE | `idata & ~IOC_TB_ENA_TEN` |\n| 241 | VALUE | `idata != 0 && idata != 0x300800000` |\n| 256 | VALUE | `idata != 0x700000` |\n| 280 | VALUE | `idata != 0x240000000` |\n| 295 | VALUE | `idata != 0x3ff00000` |\n| 315 | CATCH-ALL | `default:` -- any unimplemented ioc offset, read OR write |\n\n**THE TWO INTERRUPT STUBS ARE PLAUSIBLY THE MOST SEVERE OF THE ELEVEN, AND A ROW FRAMED AS A VALUE-GUARD CLASS\nCOULD NOT SEE THEM.**  They are not a wrong value being written -- they are an ENTIRE UNIMPLEMENTED PATH with no\npredicate at all, so *any* device asserting an interrupt through the LCA ends the process regardless of what it\nwrites.  The four \"accepts exactly one value\" registers need a firmware revision that writes something else; these\ntwo need only that an interrupt is asserted.\n\n*A row named after one class hides the members of the others.  That is this project's cell-not-class pattern\nwearing an enumeration instead of a detector -- the same shape the m88kexcdeliver chain hit four times.*\n\n**WHAT IS UNCHANGED:** the severity (HIGH), the single consumer (`machine_alpha.c`, `device_add(machine, \"lca\")`),\nthe absence of any alpha rig, and the observation that a guest here is FIRMWARE so the reaching value is whatever\nSRM or NetBSD writes.  The scope caveat about range-vs-value guards also stands and is now sharper, because there\nis only one live range guard to reason about.\n\n--- the original note follows, unaltered ---\n\n\n=== DESIGN PASS, 2026-09-06 (codex / GPT-6 Astra at max, read-only).  Answer preserved at\n=== regress/artifacts/lcabadval/codex_design_20260906.txt.  IT CORRECTED THREE OF THE FIVE PREMISES I HAD\n=== JUST RE-DERIVED, and one of the corrections changes what this round may even choose from.\n\n**SEVERITY ORDER CONFIRMED: the two INTERRUPT STUBS first** -- but \"provisionally; firmware execution order is\nunproved\", which is the honest qualifier my own framing lacked.\n\n*** A SEVENTH OPTION-SET MEMBER: IMPLEMENT. ***  The carrier's set is DELETE / DECLINE / DECLINE+LATCHED\nANNOUNCE / ACCEPT / FAULT / BACKED ACCEPT -- all six presume the access is a REFUSAL DECISION.  For the two\ninterrupt stubs none of them applies: *\"declining an interrupt leaves the machine incomplete.\"*  A stub is not a\nrefusal to service a value; it is an unimplemented PATH, and the disposition is to write the code.  This is the\noption-set completeness rule paying off exactly as it is meant to -- the member was found by a seat reading the\ntree, not by argument.\n\n*** AND THE FAULT MEMBER IS NOT AVAILABLE ON THIS MACHINE AT ALL. ***  `dev_unreadable.c` works because\n`memory_rw.c` dispatches an exception on a failed device access **for MIPS and M88K, with NO ALPHA EQUIVALENT**.\nSo the idiom this fork installs ten times in `machine_sgi.c` does not transfer here, and any round reaching for\nFAULT owes Alpha exception delivery first.  *A precedent that is real elsewhere in the tree is not thereby\ntransferable; that is the warrant-transfer rule, and it took a seat opening `memory_rw.c` to see it.*\n\n**AND A FINDING ABOUT THE ALPHA PORT THAT OUTRANKS THIS ROW:** the Alpha interrupt callbacks merely set\n`irq_asserted`, and **the execution loop never consumes that flag**.  So implementing the two stubs would not by\nitself deliver an interrupt -- removing the exits cannot complete the path.  Filed as its own question rather\nthan folded in here.\n\n## THE PREMISE AUDIT, and I was wrong three times\n\n* **PREMISE 1 HOLDS.**  Twelve textual occurrences, eleven outside comments.\n* **PREMISE 2's REMOVAL IS REAL; ITS \"USABLE PRECEDENT\" CONCLUSION IS WRONG.**  I read the commented-out\n  `dev >= 21` kill plus its live `dev = 0` as the file's own answer, transferable to this round.  It is not: it\n  **ALIASES \"no selected device\" to DEVICE ZERO**, which is harmless today only because device 7 is the sole\n  installed device and slot 0 happens to be empty.  Populate slot 0 and it is a latent alias defect.  *So the\n  question I asked -- is this a sound precedent or a latent defect of its own -- has the second answer, and my\n  brief had already leaned on the first.*\n* **PREMISE 3's CLASS COUNTS HOLD, ITS SINGLETON CLAIM DOES NOT.**  `W_BASE0` accepts **TWO** values, `0` and\n  `0x300800000` (`idata != 0ULL && idata != 0x300800000ULL`), so \"four registers accepting exactly ONE value\" is\n  wrong -- only **three** window guards accept a single value.  *This one is mine twice over: the original row\n  note said it, I re-derived the enumeration tonight and reproduced it, and the guard's own text was in my\n  terminal when I did.*\n* **PREMISE 4 HOLDS** as one machine-setup consumer (AlphaBook 1), with no dedicated boot rig -- **but\n  \"never exercised\" DOES NOT FOLLOW**: a generic constructor sweep exists (`gate_asan_sweep.sh`), without\n  proving coverage of these branches.  My \"no rig means no coverage\" was too strong.\n* **PREMISE 5 IS AN UNSUPPORTED RESTRICTION.**  I said a guest here is firmware, so the reaching value is\n  whatever SRM or NetBSD writes.  Guest KERNEL stores use the same MMIO path, and interrupt callbacks arise from\n  DEVICE events -- neither is firmware.\n\n## DISPOSITIONS IT WOULD TAKE, per class\n* **INTERRUPT STUB (2):** IMPLEMENT forwarding and CPU interrupt delivery.\n* **BUS (1):** implement Type-1 decoding then ACCEPT through `bus_pci`, using its existing absent-device\n  handling.  `ioc_conf & 1` selects the CONFIGURATION-CYCLE TYPE and is **not the decoded bus number** -- and\n  deleting this exit alone leaves `dev` uninitialised.\n* **RANGE (1):** DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE with initialised read results and no forwarded side effects.  Note\n  `ofs` is at most three, **so this guard cannot fire for four- or eight-byte accesses** -- changing `>`\n  mechanically is not a design.\n* **VALUE (6):** ACCEPT documented register fields using the existing backing, latched announce for the missing\n  DMA/TLB effects.  **Every guarded assignment already happens BEFORE its check**, so no invented backing is\n  needed -- but readback alone does not implement translation.\n* **CATCH-ALL (1):** split documented registers (`STAT0`/`STAT1`) from genuine holes; FAULT only for confirmed\n  holes and only once Alpha exception delivery exists.\n\n**WHAT IT COULD NOT ESTABLISH, said plainly:** source shows connected interrupt paths and conditional MMIO paths\nfor the other nine sites, but **no particular SRM/NetBSD execution of any terminating site**.  Firmware\nreachability and failure frequency remain unmeasured, so the severity order is provisional on exactly that.\n\n\n*** THE SEVERITY WARRANT THIS ROW WAS MISSING: THE REJECTED VALUES INCLUDE A DOCUMENTED ONE, AND THE\nSTORE LANDS BEFORE THE PROCESS ENDS.  Found by the codex seat 2026-09-07 (regress/artifacts/lcabadval/codex_scoping_20260907.txt),\nverified against the source and the header before recording. ***\n\nEach of the three window guards has this shape:\n\n    d->window_mask_0 = idata;                          <- the store LANDS\n    if (idata != 0x700000ULL) { fatal(...); exit(1); } <- then the process ends\n\n**And zero is a documented encoding.**  From src/include/thirdparty/alpha_lcareg.h:\n\n    #define IOC_W_MASK_1M   0x0000000000000000ULL   /* 1MB window */\n\nSo a guest selecting the SMALLEST DOCUMENTED WINDOW SIZE ends the emulator.  The same argument covers\nW_BASE1, whose one accepted constant 0x240000000 carries IOC_W_BASE_WEN (0x200000000, *Window enable*) --\n**so a driver DISABLING a window, by writing a base with WEN clear, is killed too.**\n\n*** THIS IS #504's WARRANT AT A SECOND DEVICE: documented values that the model kills on. ***  It is not a\njunk-value guard; it is a whitelist of ONE that excludes documented members of its own encoding.  codex adds\nthat all three registers start at zero through constructor initialisation, so the rejected value is also the\nRESET value.\n\n**AND THE RANGE SITE IS FAR MORE REACHABLE THAN THIS ROW SAID.**  It was recorded as `ofs > len'.  Reading\nDEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa): `relative_addr >>= 5; ofs = relative_addr & 3;' -- **ofs is guest ADDRESS BITS 5-6**,\nnot a written value.  So an ordinary 1-byte access at an address with those bits set to 2 or 3 ends the\nprocess.  That is address decoding, not a value whitelist, and it belongs in a different disposition class\nfrom the six value guards.  The rest of that function is filed separately as [[lcaisalane]].\n\n*** DISPOSITION PASS, codex 2026-09-07 (regress/artifacts/lcabadval/codex_disposition_20260907.txt).  IT NARROWS MY REACHABILITY CLAIM,\nCONFIRMS TWO OTHERS, AND SUPPLIES THE IN-TREE PRECEDENTS THE OPTION-SET RULE ASKS FOR -- INCLUDING\nCONFLICTING ONES, WHICH IS WHAT I ASKED IT TO SURFACE. ***\n\nVerdict: *\"Disposition 5 is unavailable through Alpha's failed-device-access path.  Five candidate rounds fit\na uniform VALUE-refusal policy.  The tree supplies CONFLICTING precedents, especially for lane handling and\nstoring unsupported values.\"*\n\n*** MY `AN ORDINARY READ-MODIFY-WRITE ENDS THE EMULATOR' CLAIM IS TOO BROAD, AND I HAVE REPEATED IT IN THREE\nPLACES. ***  codex: *\"they read zero INITIALLY, then echo accepted writes.  Writing back the initial zero\nkills the last three; an arbitrary RMW does not necessarily kill them -- its resulting value matters.\"*\n\n**The precise claim is the INITIAL-ZERO readback**: before any accepted write, `W_MASK0', `W_BASE1' and\n`W_MASK1' read 0 and accept only one non-zero constant each, so a read-modify-write performed on a\nfreshly-reset device writes 0 back and ends the process.  **Once a valid value has been written the register\nechoes it**, and an RMW of that value is accepted.  So the window is early -- driver probe or\ninitialisation -- rather than any RMW at any time.  That is still a routine thing for a driver to do at\nattach, and it is still reached with a documented encoding (`IOC_W_MASK_1M` = 0), but the sentence I wrote\nwas wider than the fact.\n\n**CONFIRMED, both of which I had asserted:** every VALUE arm STORES BEFORE CHECKING; and the accepted sets\nare exactly as filed (`IOC_CONF' {0,1}, `IOC_TB_ENA' {0,0x80}, `W_BASE0' {0,0x300800000}, and the three\nsingle-value ones).\n\n**DISPOSITION 5 IS UNAVAILABLE -- now established from a third direction.**  The carrier already recorded it\ngenerally, [[alphafaultclaim]] reached it from the instruction side, and this reaches it from the\nfailed-device-access path.  **Any proposal for a bus-error refusal on an Alpha device owes Alpha exception\ndelivery first**, which is a far larger round than the one it would be riding on.\n\n**THE PRECEDENT I ASKED FOR, AND IT IS AN EXACT ONE:** `cpu_sh.c' defines `sh3_cpu_interrupt_assert()' and\n`sh3_cpu_interrupt_deassert()' with EMPTY `/* TODO */' bodies -- *\"an exact unimplemented-callback precedent\nfor 2, ignore silently\"* -- and `core/interrupt.c' defines empty `no_interrupt_assert/deassert' callbacks,\nthough those represent intentionally absent connections rather than unimplemented ones.  **So the two\nINTERRUPT STUBS have a direct in-tree answer** and do not need disposition 7 after all, which is what I had\nassumed they needed.\n\n**AND IT REPORTS THE PRECEDENTS CONFLICT** -- especially on lane handling and on whether to store an\nunsupported value.  That is the more useful answer: it means the choice is genuinely open at those sites\nrather than settled by the tree, so a round there must argue rather than transfer.\n\n**SCOPE: FIVE candidate rounds** under a uniform VALUE-refusal policy -- consistent with the earlier read's\n*\"this needs several rounds\"* and finer than it.\n\n**WHERE THE OVER-BROAD FORM SURVIVES, named because it cannot be edited:** commit `de96f11`'s MESSAGE\nsays *\"each reads back 0 while its guard accepts one non-zero constant, and AN ORDINARY READ-MODIFY-WRITE\nENDS THE EMULATOR on all three\"*.  A commit message is immutable, so the house rule -- correct the false\nrecord IN PLACE -- cannot be followed literally.  **The next best thing is to point at it from the record a\nreader will reach**, which is this row.  The narrowed claim above is the operative one: the kill needs the\nINITIAL zero readback, not any RMW at any time.\n\n*** THE PREREQUISITE IS DISCHARGED.  `#506` LANDED THIS AFTERNOON. ***\n\nThis row's HOLD-Q asked: *\"given that every row must match the `fatal()` TEXT rather than the exit\nstatus, and given that `lcaconfwidth` means the IOC_CONF arm's text can PRINT THE WRONG VALUE, is the\ntext-matching oracle sound at THAT arm -- or must `lcaconfwidth`'s printf fix land FIRST, making it a\nprerequisite of this row rather than a sibling?\"*\n\n**It landed.**  `#506` repaired both narrowing sites in `dev_lca.c` -- `:205` (IOC_CONF) and the\n`default:` arm -- to `0x%\" PRIx64` with `(uint64_t)idata`, matching the five siblings.  Measured by\n`regress/lca_confwidth_probe.py`: **HEAD 4/6 -> fix 6/6, same script**, with `kill width` now reading\n`0x100000000` instead of `0x0`.\n\n> **So the text oracle is now sound at the IOC_CONF arm, and the ordering question in the HOLD-Q is\n> answered by events rather than by adjudication.**  What remains of that HOLD-Q is its SECOND half,\n> untouched: *the de-escalation clause owes an accept row PER PREDICATE, and the ledger's earlier\n> census dropped `W_BASE1` and `W_MASK1` -- confirm the accept-row set is six, not four.*\n\n*The interrupt-stub confound is also unchanged and still governs the witness design:\n`lca_interrupt_assert` and `lca_interrupt_deassert` are bare `fatal()`+`exit(1)` with the ISA PIC\nchain wired to them, so ANY interrupt ends the host with the same status as the value guards under\ntest.  That is why the rows must match text, which is why `#506` was the prerequisite.*\n\n*** THE HOLD-Q'S SECOND HALF IS ANSWERED BY MEASUREMENT: THE ACCEPT-ROW SET IS SIX. ***\nEnumerated by reading `if (idata` in `dev_lca.c`, 2026-09-07:\n\n    :203  IOC_CONF   idata & ~1\n    :231  TB_ENA     idata & ~IOC_TB_ENA_TEN\n    :246  W_BASE0    idata != 0ULL && idata != 0x300800000ULL\n    :261  W_MASK0    idata != 0x700000ULL\n    :285  W_BASE1    idata != 0x240000000ULL\n    :300  W_MASK1    idata != 0x3ff00000ULL\n\n**Six, not four** -- the earlier census in this row dropped `W_BASE1` and `W_MASK1`, exactly as the\nHOLD-Q suspected.  *Note two shapes, which may matter for the fix design: two BITMASK tests\n(`& ~mask`) and four EQUALITY tests, one of which accepts two values.*\n\n**And the eleven-live count is confirmed rather than carried:** `grep -cE '^[[:space:]]+exit\\(1\\);'`\ngives **12**, of which `:129` sits inside a commented-out block -- so 11 live, in **FIVE** classes:\n2 interrupt stubs, 1 bus/cycle-type, 1 range/lane, 6 value, 1 default.\n\n> #### *** THIS SENTENCE SAID \"four classes\" AND THEN LISTED FIVE.  Corrected 2026-09-07 after the\n> #### grok design pass named it -- and the same slip was in the brief that asked it to. ***\n> The opener asks seats to check enumerations; mine was wrong in its own headline, with the\n> refutation printed three words later.  *A count and the list beside it are two claims, and\n> nothing checks them against each other.*\n\n*** AND THE FIRST HALF OF THE HOLD-Q IS DISCHARGED BY EVENTS: `#506` LANDED (`7a41b3f`). ***\nThe question was whether `lcaconfwidth`'s printf fix had to land first, since this row's rows must\nmatch the `fatal()` TEXT rather than the exit status (the two interrupt stubs kill with the same\nstatus as every value guard).  **It landed and is measured** -- both narrowing sites now print\n`0x%\" PRIx64` with `(uint64_t)idata`, HEAD 4/6 -> fix 6/6 -- so the IOC_CONF arm's text is sound.\n\n**Design brief out to grok** (`regress/artifacts/lcabadval/DESIGN_brief.md`) rather than codex: the\ncodex seat refused the sibling `lcaconfwidth` census on its content filter, and a brief enumerating\neleven host kills is the same recorded trigger shape.\n\n*** GROK DESIGN PASS 2026-09-07: **NOT RATIFY** as a six-armed one-design round until two of my\nclaims are dropped.  The ROUND SHAPE survives; two warrants and one count do not. ***\n\n**1. \"THE ACCEPT-ROW SET IS SIX\" STILL UNDER-COUNTS.**  De-escalation owes a row per PREDICATE, but\nthree of the six predicates accept TWO values:\n\n    IOC_CONF  {0, 1}          TB_ENA   {0, 0x80}      W_BASE0  {0, 0x300800000}\n    W_MASK0   {0x700000}      W_BASE1  {0x240000000}  W_MASK1  {0x3ff00000}\n\n**Nine pinning values, not six rows** -- and three of the nine are the reset `0`, which is vacuous\nunless sequenced after a nonzero write.  *So replacing the old \"four\" with \"six\" corrected one\nunder-count into another.*\n\n**2. S5 DOES NOT TRANSFER, AND CITING IT WOULD CITE A DEFECT AS A WARRANT.**  I offered the live\n`dev = 0` path (`dev >= 21`, kill commented out) as this file's own *delete-the-kill, substitute-a-\nsafe-default* precedent.  **It is PCI type-0 TAG DECODE, not a register-value policy**: it aliases\n\"no device selected\" onto SLOT 0, which is empty today only because the installed SIO is device 7.\n**And that loop is already filed as [[lcashiftub]] -- `0x800 << 20` in a signed `int` at `dev == 20`\nis UB.**  Substituting `0x700000` when firmware wrote `IOC_W_MASK_1M` (0) would be that same alias\npointed at a documented encoding.\n\n> **THE RIGHT IN-FILE WARRANT IS `W_T_BASE0` / `W_T_BASE1`**: the sibling TRANSLATED-window registers\n> already store any value and do not kill.  *Base/mask killing on a firmware snapshot while\n> translated-base accepts everything is the file's own outlier* -- which is a far stronger warrant\n> than the one I proposed, and one grep away.\n\n**3. THE ROUND SHAPE IS RATIFIED: ONE ROUND, MEMBER 4 ACCEPT, ALL SIX.**  Delete `fatal`+`exit` and\nkeep the store that already ran.  Five of the six fields are pure storage (`window_base_*`,\n`window_mask_*`, `tlb_enable` appear only as `odata = d->X` / `d->X = idata`); the sixth, `ioc_conf`,\nis consumed only as `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1` in `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)`, **so extra CONF bits\nare already discarded at the only consumer** and a masked store would be an unlicensed addition.\n**Bitmask vs equality is a DETECTOR-SHAPE split, not a fix-design split.**  FAULT is unavailable on\nAlpha.\n\n*** 4. THE FLOOR-(a) TRAP, and it is the finding that most changes the detector: AN ACCEPT-ONLY\nDETECTOR IS GREEN ON HEAD. ***  Writing the six magic constants SURVIVES on the committed build.  So\na detector that writes only currently-legal values **is green under verbatim reinstatement of all six\noriginal guards** -- the vacuity-floor miss in its purest form.  **The rows that make (b) bite are the\ndocumented-REJECTED writes**: MASK0 `0`, BASE1 with WEN clear, CONF bit 32, a non-TEN TB_ENA bit.\n\n**5. THE (d) FORMS HERE ARE SITE OMISSION AND DISJUNCT-DROPPING, not an operand swap.**  Leave\n`W_MASK1` (or `W_BASE1`) fatal while de-fatalising the other five -- incomplete clause (b), and\n**`ioc rt` in `lca_confwidth_probe.py` stays green because nothing in that probe touches MASK1.**\nSecond form: on `W_BASE0`, drop `idata != 0ULL &&` and keep the magic comparison -- the magic accept\nrow stays green, *which is exactly why BASE0 owes TWO pins*.\n\n**6. `lca_confwidth_probe.py`'s `kill fired` and `died` WANTS WILL INVERT IF THIS ROUND SHIPS.**  It\nis a `#506` witness, not this round's detector.  **Re-pin it under the oracle-correction rule -- do\nnot launder by editing wants after the product change.**\n\n**7. The round now carries an open A-vs-B design question (keep the store vs decline the write) that the W_T_BASE* siblings CANNOT settle, since accepting-anything there may be an absent guard rather than a decision.  Disposition set also grew by two source-backed members (accept/decline WITH REPEATED announcements; complete with a GUEST-VISIBLE DEVICE ERROR, dev_wdc.c's WDCE_ABRT+interrupt). W_T_BASE0 (:272) and W_T_BASE1 (:311) confirmed UNGUARDED: plain readback/store, no value test -- which is the sibling warrant the design brief puts to the panel.\n\n*** AND THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE WAS ITSELF CORRUPTED WHEN FIRST WRITTEN -- BY THE SHELL, QUIETLY\nENOUGH TO SURVIVE REVIEW. ***  It was applied through `python -c \"...\"` with DOUBLE quotes, and\nbash performed COMMAND SUBSTITUTION on the backticks inside it: `exit(1)` and `default:` were\nEXECUTED and replaced by their empty output, leaving readable prose with two words silently\nmissing -- \"Derived, not recalled:  occurs SEVEN times\".  bash did print two errors, but python\nran on the mangled source anyway and the write SUCCEEDED, so the record was wrong while the\ncommand reported success.\n\n**Inside a double-quoted shell argument a backtick is a COMMAND, not a quote mark.**  That is the\ncarrier's backslash-through-heredoc class arriving on a new character, and with the same\nsignature: the failure lands in the FILE, not in the exit status.\n\n*** AND THE REPAIR FAILED TWICE MORE BEFORE IT LANDED, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING. ***  The\nfix was retried as `python -c '...'` with SINGLE quotes -- correct for backticks -- and bash\nrejected it, because the note explaining the hazard contains the word \"carrier's\", whose\nAPOSTROPHE terminated the shell quote.  So: double quotes lose to backticks, single quotes lose to\napostrophes, and English prose about shell quoting reliably contains both.\n\n**The rule is not a better quoting style.  It is: PROSE DESTINED FOR A RECORD GOES INTO A SCRIPT\nFILE, never through `-c`.**  This paragraph was written that way and landed intact on the first\nattempt.\n\n*** PASS-1 DESIGN PANEL 2026-09-07 (preserved: regress/artifacts/lcabadval/panel_p1_20260907/ -- _scratchpad is never committed). That is checkable and should be checked before either\nis adopted.\n\n**THE DISSENT, recorded as a dissent and not as noise:** deepseek alone says premise 6 is unsound\nfor all six -- *\"the guard's accepted-value set is the model's CONTRACT. The existing lie for accepted values does not license\nexpanding the set of lies.\"*  It also warns the reasoning generalises: it would justify removing any\nstore-before-guard exit anywhere.  **That generalisation objection is the strongest single argument\nagainst A and is not answered by the five-versus-one split**, because it attacks the warrant rather\nthan its scope.\n\n**A BRIEF DEFECT OF MINE, named by glm and correct:** premise 2's field counts were *asserted, not\ninlined* -- I ran the greps and wrote the conclusion instead of the OUTPUT. Premise 3 stands and masking would be an addition without a\nwarrant.\n\n*** 8. THE WITNESS IS NOT PENDING -- THE RECIPE EXISTS.  This moves the round off documented-only. ***\n* **Machine: `alphabook1` ONLY.**  `machine_alpha.c`'s `device_add(machine, \"lca\")` is under\n  `ST_ALPHABOOK1` and no other subtype.\n* **Addresses** (K0SEG | physical, because `alpha_translate_v2p` identity-maps K0SEG in kernel mode):\n  CONF `0xfffffc0180000020`, TB_ENA `...00a0`, BASE0 `...0100`, BASE1 `...0120`, MASK0 `...0140`,\n  MASK1 `...0160`.\n* **`regress/lca_confwidth_probe.py` ALREADY issues a guest `stq`/`ldq` through this exact decode at\n  CONF on unmodified `alphabook1`**; the other five are further cases of the same DEVICE_ACCESS on\n  the same mapping.\n* **Three traps that file already paid for, so a new probe need not rediscover them:** debugger\n  `dump` skips `DM_DEFAULT` handlers; a debugger `put d` of a killing value IS a host kill but is\n  NOT a guest instruction (so it does not witness); and HAE at window offset 0 silently succeeds, so\n  **a miss control must not use `LCA_IOC_BASE+0`**.\n\n**9. One DESIGN still covers all six (delete the\nexit, keep the store, optional latch); one design does NOT if you pick B, because *\"B is a coherent\nanswer only for a consumed field, which five of these are not.\"*\n\n*** THE codex SEAT LANDED LAST AND OVERTURNED THE PANEL ON A FACT CHECKABLE IN ONE GREP.  MY\nPREMISE 6 IS DEAD, AND SO IS THE FORMULATION I HAD CREDITED grok WITH. ***\n\n**codex's verdict:** *\"Your argument for A fails as written: RCR1 also has no functional consumer.\nI recommend B for pass 1 as a conservative policy choice; hardware-correct write/readback behavior\nremains unproved.\"*\n\n**VERIFIED HERE, not taken on trust** -- `grep -rn rtc_rcr1 src/` returns exactly FOUR occurrences:\n\n    dev_sh4.c:388    uint8_t   rtc_rcr1;              declaration\n    dev_sh4.c:2776   odata = d->rtc_rcr1;             READBACK\n    dev_sh4.c:2781   ...landed in rtc_rcr1 and...     INSIDE THE COMMENT\n    dev_sh4.c:2805   d->rtc_rcr1 = idata;             STORE\n\n**Zero functional consumers.**  And the comparison that settles it: `ioc_conf` also has four\noccurrences, but its fourth is `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1` -- a real consumer.  **So RCR1 is consumed\nLESS than LCA's CONF, not more.**\n\n*** WHAT THIS DESTROYS. ***  My premise 6 claimed LCA differs from RCR1 because *\"RCR1's stored value\nhas a meaning the model otherwise honours\"*.  It has no such meaning: the model stores it and reads\nit back, exactly like the five LCA window fields.  **grok's formulation, which I recorded above as\n\"the keeper\" -- *\"RCR1 refuses CONSUMED FEATURE BITS; these guards whitelist UNIMPLEMENTED\nCONSTANTS\"* -- is FALSE IN ITS FIRST HALF.**  So is my own gloss that `#447`'s edit \"does not\ntransfer\".\n\n**IT TRANSFERS EXACTLY.**  `#447` faced a store-before-guard field with NO functional consumer,\nand chose to MOVE THE STORE BELOW THE GUARD.  That is the LCA shape, arm for arm.  The tree's own\nprecedent is therefore evidence FOR B, which is what codex, deepseek and (for CONF) glm all said.\n\n*** AND THE SURVIVING ARGUMENT IS THE ONE deepseek RAISED FIRST AND codex SHARPENED: THE GUEST IS\nITSELF A CONSUMER OF READBACK. ***  codex: *\"Previously, a rejected write ended execution before\nsubsequent guest code could observe that value.  A creates that observation and allows the guest to\nmake further decisions from it.  Existing incomplete behavior for accepted settings does not\nestablish that these newly admitted executions are equally harmless.\"*  That argument is untouched by\nthe consumer census, because it does not depend on a MODEL consumer at all -- and it is precisely\nwhat `#447`'s comment means by *\"the guest writes CIE|AIE, reads CIE|AIE back, and believes the RTC\nwill interrupt it.\"*\n\n**TALLY, and the argument decides it rather than the count:** codex -> B; deepseek -> B; glm -> B for\nCONF and not settleable for the five; kimi substitute -> split per arm; grok -> A; minimax -> A.  But\ngrok's and minimax's A both rest on the consumed/unconsumed distinction, **and that distinction is\nnow measured false.**  Count the arguments, not the seats: the load-bearing premise under the A\nposition has been refuted by a grep.\n\n**THIS IS THE THIRD REVERSAL IN THIS ROUND'S DESIGN AND IT COST NOTHING, WHICH IS THE POINT OF THE\nHOLD DISCIPLINE.**  No code is committed; the round has produced adjudication text and zero product\nchanges.  The earlier records above -- premise 6 \"sound for five arms\", grok's formulation as \"the\nkeeper\" -- are left standing WITH this correction beneath them rather than silently edited, because\na future reader needs to see that the panel's majority reasoning was overturned and by what.\n\n*** WHAT IS NOW OWED BEFORE ANY FIX: the ARM-BY-ARM design, with the RCR1 precedent read as\nSUPPORTING B, and codex's readback-consumer argument answered rather than assumed away.  The\nquestion that remains genuinely open is minimax's measurable one -- under B a rejected CONF write\nretains the previous value, and codex adds that W_MASK0/W_BASE1/W_MASK1 RESET TO ZERO, which is NOT\nin their accepted set, so \"retain the last accepted value\" is not even well-defined at reset.  That\nis a concrete defect in B as stated and it needs an answer (retain? force the reset value? decline\nonly the guarded bits?) before B can ship. ***\n\n*** AND THE SHARPEST LESSON IS NOT THE REVERSAL, IT IS HOW MY GLOSS BEAT MY OWN PRIMARY SOURCE. ***\n\nThe brief inlined #447s RCR1 comment VERBATIM.  That comment argues purely from READBACK:\n\n    the guest writes CIE|AIE, reads CIE|AIE back, and believes the RTC will\n    interrupt it.  It never will.\n\n**It never claims a model consumer.**  The consumed/unconsumed framing is MINE -- premise 6 glossed\nit as RCR1s stored value having a meaning the model otherwise honours -- and grok adopted my\ngloss rather than the quoted text sitting directly above it, producing B copies RCR1s edit\nwithout RCR1s reason.  codex went to the source, grepped the field, and found the gloss false.\n\n> **INLINING THE PRIMARY SOURCE DOES NOT PROTECT A BRIEF FROM A WRONG SUMMARY PLACED BESIDE IT.**\n> A seat reads the summary as the QUESTION and the quote as SUPPORTING MATERIAL, so a false gloss\n> steers the answer even when the refuting text is on the same page.  This projects premise-audit\n> rule says to state load-bearing premises AS QUESTIONS; the refinement is that a premise which\n> RESTATES an inlined quotation is the most dangerous kind, because it looks already verified.\n\n*The cheap defence is the one codex used and I did not: when a premise characterises what some\nother code does, GREP THAT CODE.  `grep -rn rtc_rcr1 src/` is four lines of output and it was\navailable to me before the brief went out.*\n\n*** codex, `#511` PASS 2, CHALLENGES THE \"GRACEFUL DEGRADATION PRECEDENT\" THAT BOTH LCA ROWS LEAN\nON. ***\n\n*\"`DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)`'s fallback selects device zero and forwards the access through\n`bus_pci_setaddr`.  **That is an ALIASING DECISION, not by itself proof of harmless degradation.**\nThe comment's 'transferable warrant' needs its own justification; the shift repair does not establish\nit.\"*\n\nThe `dev >= 21` arm -- commented-out `fatal()`/`exit(1)` with `dev = 0;` substituted -- has been\ncited repeatedly here, including by [[lcabadval]], as the file's own in-tree warrant for deleting a\nhost kill.  **codex's point is that substituting device ZERO is not a decline: it silently redirects\nthe access to a DIFFERENT DEVICE.**  Whether that is harmless is a separate question nobody has\nasked, and \"someone already did it in this file\" is not the same as \"it is right\".\n\n> **A TRANSFERRED WARRANT NEEDS ITS OWN JUSTIFICATION, NOT JUST A PRECEDENT.**  This is the\n> option-set rule's own caution turned around: grepping the fork for an existing idiom finds what the\n> tree DOES, which is evidence about practice and not about correctness.\n\n*Not resolved here.  Recorded on both rows because both cite it, and any round that leans on it owes\nthe justification codex is asking for.*\n\n*** THE THIRD OPTION-SET MEMBER IS NAMED, AND THE TWO-OPTION FRAMING WAS REALLY A ONE-OPTION\nFRAMING.  codex, 2026-09-08; the two load-bearing claims VERIFIED here before recording. ***\n\n**VERDICT: C -- decline the out-of-set write, PRESERVE the previous register value, announce once.**\n\n### 1.  A AND B OVERLAP.  There was never a choice between them.\n\n*\"Your reconstructed A and B overlap: both store the written value and continue.  With the existing\nreads retained, B describes SHADOW STORAGE WITH READBACK, not a write-only register.  It supplies no\ndistinct state-transition policy.\"*  **So the panel that argued A-versus-B was arguing about one\noption under two names** -- the option-set-completeness failure in its purest form, and this row had\nit for two rounds.\n\n### 2.  *** WHAT `#447` ACTUALLY DOES IS DECLINE-AND-PRESERVE, AND I HAD IT BACKWARDS. ***\n\nThe precedent was cited as \"a register with no consumer, where the write is accepted\".  The\nimplementation says otherwise -- **the store is in the ELSE branch**:\n\n    if (idata & (SH_RCR1_CIE | SH_RCR1_AIE)) {\n            /* bounded diagnostic */\n    } else\n            d->rtc_rcr1 = idata;\n\n*\"Unsupported writes preserve the previous value.  They are NOT accepted into backing storage.\"*  And\nthe reason recorded in that comment is a READBACK concern -- echoing interrupt-enable flags would\nsuggest functionality the model does not provide -- so **absence of a consumer neither disqualifies\ndecline-and-preserve nor argues for accepting everything**: readback is guest-observable on its own.\n\n### 3.  *** `ioc_conf` HAS A FUNCTIONAL CONSUMER.  VERIFIED. ***\n\n`dev_lca.c:121` is `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1;`, feeding the PCI decode.  **The row's \"no functional\nconsumer\" premise is false for CONF**, whatever it is for the other five.\n\n> **AND IT TIES TWO HIGH ROWS TOGETHER.**  `lcaconfbus1kill` is precisely about that consumer: a\n> write of `0x1` makes `bus == 1` and arms the `bus != 0` host kill.  `0x1` is INSIDE this arm's\n> accepted set `{0, 1}`, so **C does not protect against it and must not be described as if it\n> does.**  The two rows are separate defects on one register and both need fixing.\n\n### 4.  THE RESET QUESTION IS DISSOLVED, NOT ANSWERED -- and the reason improves the fix.\n\n*\"The predicates govern WRITES, not all possible stored states.\"*  Initialization assigns zero with\nno guard; reads return the stored value with no guard; **and the write assigns BEFORE the guard is\nevaluated.**  So the model can return zero at reset while refusing a later write of zero:\n*\"that asymmetry is established; it is not a logical contradiction.\"*\n\n    register  writes the guard accepts        reset 0 passes?\n    CONF      {0, 1}                          yes\n    TB_ENA    {0, 0x80}                       yes\n    W_BASE0   {0, 0x300800000}                yes\n    W_MASK0   {0x700000}                      NO\n    W_BASE1   {0x240000000}                   NO\n    W_MASK1   {0x3ff00000}                    NO\n\n*** MY OWN ADDITION, from verifying the store-order claim at `:233` vs the guard at `:236`: BECAUSE\nTHE STORE HAPPENS FIRST, SIMPLY DELETING THE `exit(1)` LEAVES THE BAD VALUE STORED. ***  That is not\na weaker version of C -- it is a DIFFERENT OBSERVABLE STATE, readable by the guest through the\nexisting read arm.  **C requires moving each store behind its guard, exactly as RCR1 does.**\n\n### 5.  THE SIBLING EVIDENCE SUPPORTS BOTH POLICIES AND SELECTS NEITHER\n\nInside this same switch:\n\n    LCA_IOC_BASE, LCA_IOC_TBIA      discard writes; reads return 0; success\n    LCA_IOC_W_T_BASE0/1             store writes and return them on reads\n    default:                        diagnose and TERMINATE, no backing storage\n\n*\"Thus the sibling evidence supports BOTH discarding writes and retaining shadow state.  It does not\nselect a universal policy.\"*  **A useful negative result: the grep-the-fork rule found the member but\ncannot settle the policy here**, because the file is internally inconsistent.\n\n*`default:` is explicitly OUT of this round -- it has no previous value to preserve, so C is not even\nexpressible there.*\n\n### 6.  THE CENSUS SURVIVES ITS AUDIT\n\nEleven live explicit `exit(1)`: the six value arms at **248, 268, 283, 298, 322, 337**, plus\n**73, 85, 167, 196, 357**.  A bare `exit(` grep returns thirteen; two are comments (the explanatory\nmention and the disabled call whose live fallback is `dev = 0`).  *This is the row's third census and\nthe first to survive.\n\n*** CORRECTION, WITHIN THE HOUR AND BEFORE THIS PROPAGATED: \"THE THIRD OPTION-SET MEMBER\" IS WRONG.\nTHERE IS NO THIRD MEMBER.  THE OPTION SET WAS ALREADY RIGHT AND **MY BRIEF CORRUPTED IT**. ***\n\n`tools/pipeline/fable_queue.md` §7i states the fork exactly as the earlier panel left it:\n\n    A  delete fatal()+exit(1) from all six arms, KEEP the store that already ran\n    B  DECLINE -- move the store BELOW the guard, as #447 did for SH4's RCR1\n\n**The queue's B IS codex's C.**  Decline-and-preserve was already ON the table, correctly described,\nwith the `#447` precedent already attached to it. Derived 2026-09-08 by opening the dispatcher. ***\n\n`src/cpus/memory_rw.c`, the device-failure path -- the whole of it:\n\n    if (res <= 0 && !no_exceptions) {\n            debug(\"[ %s device '%s' addr %08lx failed ]\");\n    #ifdef MEM_MIPS\n            mips_cpu_exception(cpu, cache == CACHE_INSTRUCTION? The size of each window is programmed to be from 1 MB to 4 GB ...  Table\n> 6-6 shows the supported values of IOC_W_MASK register bits <31:20>; **other values are\n> unsupported**.\"*\n\n**Table 6-6 (`:9581-9598`) has THIRTEEN rows** -- a doubling run from 1 MB (`0x000`) to 4 GB\n(`0xfff`), each a contiguous run of low ones.\n\n**MY DERIVATION, flagged as mine:** the register value is the field shifted left 20, so the fork's\ntwo accepted constants are\n\n    LCA_IOC_W_MASK0   accepts ONLY 0x700000     ->  field 0x007  ->    8 MB   legal, 1 of 13\n    LCA_IOC_W_MASK1   accepts ONLY 0x3ff00000   ->  field 0x3ff  ->    1 GB   legal, 1 of 13\n\nand the legal set is exactly `(f & (f + 1)) == 0`, verified over all thirteen.\n\n> **IF THAT IS RIGHT, the model terminates the host on TWELVE DOCUMENTED-LEGAL VALUES PER MASK\n> REGISTER -- 24 across the two -- and a guest programming any window size other than 8 MB / 1 GB\n> is doing something the hardware manual explicitly supports.**  That is a much sharper severity\n> statement than *\"rejects values outside a small accepted set\"*, because it names the denominator.\n\n*** IT IS NOT RECORDED AS ESTABLISHED, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE. ***  The load-bearing step is my own\narithmetic, done in one sitting, on a table extracted by `pdftotext` -- and this project's rule is\nthat an extracted table can stagger names against values.  **Table 6-6 LOOKS self-calibrating (a\ndoubling bit-run beside a doubling size run, thirteen against thirteen), but the calibration rule\nsays one point is only valid for a UNIFORM stagger and you cannot tell which kind you have from one\npoint.**  So it went out to a panel with *\"check the premises, not just the derivations\"* and with\nthe derivation shown step by step:\n\n    regress/artifacts/lcabadval/MANUAL_brief.md   file-reading seats: codex, grok -- also asked to\n                                                  read S6.4 for the OTHER FOUR arms and to give a\n                                                  count WITH ITS DENOMINATOR per W_MASK and (as far as I read) NOT for W_BASE, so the two registers may need different\ndispositions; `0x300800000` and `0x240000000` are 34-bit values and I have not established how wide\nthese registers are or what bits above 31 mean; and the more dangerous error direction is a real\nquestion -- terminating on a legal value, versus accepting a value the model's own address decode\nthen does not honour.\n\n*** THE PANEL ANSWERED: SIX SEATS, MY COUNT CONFIRMED -- AND THE ONE SEAT THAT DISSENTED MADE THE\nDEFECT BIGGER, NOT SMALLER. ***\n\n    codex     CONFIRMS 12/13 per register, 24/26 register-value pairs, and Table 6-6 IS\n              self-calibrating -- it parsed all thirteen rows and checked the adjacent runs\n    glm       \"the S3 arithmetic is right -- I re-derived every step and found no defect\"\n    kimi      \"your derivation is right\": shift, both fields, the count, the invariant\n    minimax   \"the S3 derivation is correct\"\n    grok      answered, 14,818 b\n    deepseek  CORRECT ONLY IF bits <19:0> must be zero -- AND THAT PREMISE IS NOT ESTABLISHED\n              BY THE TEXT I QUOTED\n\n*** deepseek IS RIGHT, AND codex SETTLED IT FROM THE OTHER END. ***  My quote enumerates bits\n<31:20> and says nothing about <19:0>.  codex read the preface: **RES fields are type `X` --\nIGNORED ON WRITES, unpredictable on reads, NOT must-be-zero** (`21066_66a_hrm.txt:955`, `:985`).\n\n> **SO THE GUARD IS WORSE THAN \"ACCEPTS ONE OF THIRTEEN\".  IT TESTS THE WHOLE 64-BIT OPERAND\n> AGAINST A SINGLE LITERAL -- `if (idata != 0x700000ULL)` -- WHILE THE HARDWARE DEFINES A 12-BIT\n> FIELD AND IGNORES THE REST.**  A guest writing `0x7000ff`, functionally identical on real\n> hardware, terminates the emulator.  *deepseek said so in as many words: \"if lower bits are\n> ignored, the number of legal register values is much larger than 13 per mask, and the code is\n> even more restrictive.\"*\n\n**AND IT SUPPLIED THE TRAP A NAIVE FIX WOULD HIT:** the invariant must be applied to the SHIFTED\nFIELD.  `(idata & (idata + 1)) == 0` on the full value **REJECTS THE LEGAL `0x700000`**, because\n`0x700000 & 0x700001 == 0x700000`.  The guard is `f = idata >> 20; (f & (f + 1)) == 0`.\n\n*** FOUR MORE CORRECTIONS FROM codex, EACH NARROWING OR SHARPENING A CLAIM OF MINE. ***\n\n* **MY OWN TABLE DROPPED A ROW.**  `MANUAL_brief.md` reformatted Table 6-6 into three columns and\n  **lost `0000 0001 1111` = 32 MB** -- twelve rows under a sentence saying thirteen.  The PACKET\n  brief has all thirteen.  *A transcription error in the brief that asks others to check my\n  transcription.*\n* **ALIGNMENT AND NON-OVERLAP, which my quote stopped one paragraph short of** (`:9618`, S6.4.9):\n  bases must align naturally to window size and windows must not overlap.  **With the two accepted\n  bases held FIXED, window 0 admits only the four sizes through 8 MB and window 1 the eleven\n  through 1 GB** -- so in that configuration the guards reject 3/4 and 10/11.  All thirteen sizes\n  are supported; changing size can require changing the surrounding configuration.\n* **`IOC_CONF` IS A TWO-BIT FIELD, values 0..3, so `idata & ~1` rejects 2 of 4** defined settings --\n  I had quoted the guard without checking the field width.\n* **`IOC_TB_ENA` IS NOT A FUNCTIONAL DEFECT AT ALL: TEN has exactly two states and BOTH are\n  accepted -- 0/2.**  Its only defect is the ignored-bit one.  *Recorded because a round that\n  \"fixes six arms\" would be overclaiming.*\n\n*** THE SHARPEST SINGLE FINDING, AND IT IS NOT MINE: THE MANUAL REQUIRES INITIALISING BOTH BASE\nREGISTERS TO ZERO (S6.4.14, `:10788`), AND `LCA_IOC_W_BASE1` REJECTS EXACTLY THAT. ***  A guest\nfollowing the manual own documented initialisation sequence terminates the emulator.  That is not a\ncorner value -- it is the prescribed setup.\n\n**Also established:** the registers are **64-bit, quadword access only** (S6.4 excludes longword,\n`:10175`); and **all six arms STORE THE VALUE BEFORE TESTING IT** (`d->window_mask_0 = idata;`\nprecedes the guard), so device state is already updated when the process dies.\n\n**DISPOSITION IS NOT SETTLED HERE.**  deepseek proposes accept-with-backing-storage for the values\nthe manual or existing code already treats as legal, plus a `(once)` decline otherwise, and warns\nagainst expanding the W_BASE set without evidence since the manual enumerates none.  That is a\ndesign question for the round, and the round must not silently widen an accept set.\n\n*** THE FIX DESIGN IS IN, AND IT QUALIFIES THREE OF THE ASSESS PANEL'S FINDINGS -- ONE OF THEM A\nCONCLUSION I HAD ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN. ***\n\n**VERDICT (codex):** *preserve masked register state; validate `W_MASK` FIELDS; remove the\n`W_BASE` magic-value whitelists; leave TEN's two-state policy alone but correct `TB_ENA`'s\nreserved-bit guard.*  **\"This fixes register handling, not DMA translation\"** -- the scope\nsentence the round must carry.\n\n* *** `TB_ENA` IS NOT EXEMPT AFTER ALL, AND MY RECORD SAID IT WAS. ***  I recorded *\"IOC_TB_ENA is\n  NOT a functional defect at all -- TEN has exactly two states and both are accepted (0/2)\"*.  That\n  is right **only for normalised TEN values**: the arm's other bits are `RES`, type `X`, so the\n  existing whole-operand guard **still rejects writes the hardware ignores**.  Leaving the arm\n  untouched would contradict the very answer given to Q2.  *A per-field finding restated as a\n  per-arm exemption -- the dropped-qualifier class again.*\n* **ALL SIX REGISTERS ARE WRITE-ONLY, not RW.**  The manual makes reads of IOC write-only registers\n  UNPREDICTABLE, so **preserving readback is a useful existing emulator convention, not a hardware\n  requirement.**  A round that \"accepts with backing storage\" should say which of the two it is\n  doing.\n* **AND A PRECISION CORRECTION TO HOW I STATED THE RES RULE:** *\"`RES` alone does not mean ignored\n  -- the decisive evidence is each register table's type `X`, interpreted through the\n  conventions.\"*  My record leaned on \"RES fields are type X\" as one fact; it is two, and only the\n  second carries the ignored-on-write conclusion.\n* **`IOC_CONF` has four REPRESENTABLE field encodings**, and calling them four *supported PCI\n  operating modes* goes beyond the evidence -- the HRM also defers to the PCI specification's\n  configuration-cycle encoding restrictions.  So the \"2 of 4 rejected\" figure is a count of\n  representable encodings, not of legal modes.\n\n**Table 6-6's thirteen entries and the rest of the numerical premises are re-confirmed.**\n\n*** THE SHARP FINDING IS NOT THE OFFSET COUNT.  IT IS THAT TWO DOCUMENTED REGISTERS HAVE NO CASE,\nAND ONE OF THEM IS THE ERROR-STATUS REGISTER A DRIVER READS AFTER A FAILED PROBE.  2026-09-08. ***\n\nA codex ANALYSIS job (the construction-shaped brief is refused; see [[codexcyberflag]]) enumerated\nthe `lca_ioc' region and its stream ended before a verdict. The contract as written ("Catches: the walk bound reverted or off by one, ...") doesn't literally claim to catch field collapse, but Q2's premise says the enumeration includes those fields with joint cells. So: SHIP WITH REPAIR — the repair is the detector (add the both-pending rows to session C, raising to 23-24 rows), not the diff. The diff ships as-is.
.\regress\artifacts\lunaabsentcpu\pass2_panel_20260906_094854\kimi.txt:153:  So M_IRQA0 = 22/22 PASS, and it is NOT equivalent — on real hardware semantics: two simultaneous software interrupts to cpu 0 and cpu 1: with the mutant, whichever slot is walked first latches the shared irqActive[0] and the other never asserts → a guest-visible dropped IPI on a 2-cpu machine. The detector has no both-pending cell. This is a genuine floor hole, distinct from the disclosed M_NULLAB equivalence (which is truly equivalent) and from disclosed M_NOREASSERT debt.

Under M_NULLCHECK as described: "a `!= NULL` check that skips the assert but still sets irqActive". So the assert branch becomes `if (!d->irqActive[cpu]) { if (ptr != NULL) INTERRUPT_ASSERT(...); } d->irqActive[cpu] = 1;`. Then deassert branch: `if (d->irqActive[cpu]) INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(...)` — unchanged, no NULL check → dies on disarm. If the mutant also NULL-checked the deassert, then irqActive toggles with no delivery — indistinguishable from (a) on a 1-cpu machine except... readback: identical. So a full-NULL-check mutant (skip both) is observationally equivalent to (a) on 1 cpu, and on 2 cpu... under `-n 2` both deliver. Difference appears only with hotplug (nonexistent). So such a mutant is equivalent — fine, not a real concern; but then why prefer (a)? The warrant for (a) over NULL-check: house idiom?
