SAME SEMANTIC CLASS fails: `sgidewidth` is the WIDTH class with a real accept set `{4,8}` a decline must PRESERVE, while `sgireunmapped` is the BADOFFSET class where nothing legal reaches the arm at all.  **SAME DETECTOR / NO NEW ORACLE fails, and this is the one that decides it:** `sgi_de`'s registers are READABLE so its rows read back through `odata` -- a real device-signature channel -- while `sgi_re`'s else arm has NO BACKING STORE and its rows can only observe the diagnostic and the guest-gets-zero channel.  *Those are different oracles in kind, and the tempting collapse -- 'both are a refused access ending the host' -- is exactly what the no-new-oracle clause exists to refuse.*  *** THE CHANNEL FINDING, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE IN THIS BLOCK. ***  `#497`'s `d_returnzero` escaped 35/35 and the reason generalises to BOTH these rounds: **the `(once)` announce prints IDENTICALLY for a correct decline and for a `return 0`**, because the announce happens before the return statement.  So the DIAGNOSTIC channel does not kill it either -- and neither does host survival, since a guest bus error leaves the host alive.  **The only discriminator is what the GUEST RECEIVED.**  Every round in this family owes a SENTINEL-SEEDED READ ROW: seed the destination register, perform the declined read, and assert it reads back 0 -- a correct decline delivers zero, `return 0` faults and the register keeps its seed.  *That is the row `#497` was missing, and the measuring seat reached the same repair independently while this adjudication was being written.*  *** AND THE OBVIOUS FIX IS WRONG HERE, WHICH IS THE ROUND'S REAL DESIGN CONTENT. ***  `#497`'s decline splices to the common tail, and copying that here would ship a NEW defect.  **`DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_de)`'s tail is not inert:** it reads `if (startFlag) draw_primitive(cpu, d);` and only THEN does the read writeback -- and `startFlag = relative_addr & CRIME_DE_START` is computed for **any** access, malformed ones included.  Verified by opening the file.  Today `exit(1)` fires BEFORE the switch and before `draw_primitive`, so a malformed access never draws.  **A naive fall-through would run `draw_primitive` over STALE `de_reg` state on a bad-width store carrying bit `0x800` -- a framebuffer-corrupting side effect the kill currently prevents.**  The fix would introduce a defect the defect was preventing.  **THE CORRECT FORM IS A DELIBERATE PARTIAL TAIL:** latch-warn once, then `if (writeflag == MEM_READ) memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata); return 1;` -- skipping the switch AND the draw, while still delivering the defined zero the sketch rule requires.  **Say this in the brief**, or a seat proposes the naive splice by analogy with `#497` and it looks right.  **ONE latch bit**, not two: the kill is a single direction-agnostic message (`sgi_de: len = %i not implemented`).  Contrast `sgireunmapped`, which needs two -- the count follows the MESSAGE count, not the direction count.  **The de-escalation accept clause FULLY APPLIES** and its rows are strong here because the registers are readable: `len==4` and `len==8`, read and write, still land and read back through `odata`, each reddening under the least-observable growth form.  **No `regnr` OOB risk from the decline itself** -- the register access lives inside the `len==8`/`len==4` arms, so a bad width already skips it.  *** MY SEVERITY ESCALATION WAS FALSE, AND THE ROW'S ORIGINAL FRAMING WAS RIGHT. So a width-w access arriving at the device is w-ALIGNED.  `sgi_de` is registered at `baseaddr + 0x2000` (page-aligned) for `DEV_SGI_DE_LENGTH` 0x1000 (a page multiple), so a w-aligned offset is a multiple of w and the largest is `0x1000 - w`, giving `paddr + w == 0x1000`, which is NOT `> length`.  **TRUNCATION NEVER FIRES FOR THIS DEVICE -- enumerated: ZERO truncating accesses.**  **CORRECTED ARITHMETIC:** the device sees only len in {1,2,4,8}; {4,8} are accepted; the declined set is **6144** accesses -- 4096 byte (`sb`/`lb`, any offset) and 2048 halfword (`sh`/`lh`, 2-aligned) -- of which **3072** carry startFlag. Found by the adjudicating seat while ranking the OTHER four sgi_re rows; verified here by opening the file and deriving the gap arithmetic. ***  **THE SITE** is the final `else` of the window cascade in `DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_re)` -- a `fatal()` for each direction and then a bare `exit(1)`, with **NO WIDTH PREDICATE AT ALL**.  Every other kill in this handler is reached only by choosing an unusual access WIDTH (the six `len != 8` arms) or a DIRECTION (the six read arms).  This one needs neither: any width, either direction, any unmatched address.  **THE DOOR, derived rather than asserted.**  `memory_device_register` covers `baseaddr + 0x1000` for `DEV_SGI_RE_LENGTH` = `0x1000`, and the handler restores the full offset with `relative_addr += 0x1000`, so the dispatched range is `0x1000-0x1fff`.  The six windows occupy `0x1000-0x17ff` with one internal gap:      TLB_A 0x1000-0x11ff   TLB_B 0x1200-0x13ff   TLB_C 0x1400-0x15ff      TEX   0x1600-0x16df   LINEAR_A 0x1700-0x177f  LINEAR_B 0x1780-0x17ff  leaving **`0x16e0-0x16ff` (32 bytes) plus `0x1800-0x1fff` (2048 bytes) = 2080 bytes** that fall through to the kill.  Guest KSEG1: a single `lb` at `0xb5001800` ends the host process.  *** IT SURVIVES BOTH ROUNDS THAT LOOK LIKE THEY WOULD FIX IT, WHICH IS WHY IT MUST BE FILED BEFORE THE NEXT COMPLETENESS SENTENCE SHIPS. ***  `#497` repairs the six WIDTH guards and never reaches this arm.  `sgirekills` as scoped repairs the six READ arms inside the windows and never reaches it either.  A reader of either round record could reasonably conclude the device no longer kills on a stray access.  It does.  **AND ITS FIX CANNOT BE ACCEPT**, which is what keeps it out of `sgirekills`' round rather than a scheduling preference: nothing backs an unmapped address, so there is no stored value to return.  It is DECLINE + a latched `(once)` announce -- a different fix design, so the round-scope rule's same-fix-design arm fails.  *The agy audit listed this arm as '1 unhandled offset fallback' and never asked whether it was reachable.  A census that enumerates a site without ranking it is how the widest door stays open.*  *** DESIGN SETTLED 2026-08-30 BY ADJUDICATION, BEFORE EITHER ROUND STARTS, SO NEITHER BURNS ITS PANEL ARGUING SCOPE. ***  **TWO ROUNDS, and both separate from `#497` -- all THREE round-scope conjuncts fail, worked through rather than asserted.**  SAME DEVICE fails: `sgi_de` and `sgi_re` are separate `memory_device_register` registrations at `baseaddr+0x2000` and `+0x1000`.  SAME SEMANTIC CLASS fails: `sgidewidth` is the WIDTH class with a real accept set `{4,8}` a decline must PRESERVE, while `sgireunmapped` is the BADOFFSET class where nothing legal reaches the arm at all.  **SAME DETECTOR / NO NEW ORACLE fails, and this is the one that decides it:** `sgi_de`'s registers are READABLE so its rows read back through `odata` -- a real device-signature channel -- while `sgi_re`'s else arm has NO BACKING STORE and its rows can only observe the diagnostic and the guest-gets-zero channel.  *Those are different oracles in kind, and the tempting collapse -- 'both are a refused access ending the host' -- is exactly what the no-new-oracle clause exists to refuse.*  *** THE CHANNEL FINDING, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE IN THIS BLOCK. ***  `#497`'s `d_returnzero` escaped 35/35 and the reason generalises to BOTH these rounds: **the `(once)` announce prints IDENTICALLY for a correct decline and for a `return 0`**, because the announce happens before the return statement.  So the DIAGNOSTIC channel does not kill it either -- and neither does host survival, since a guest bus error leaves the host alive.  **The only discriminator is what the GUEST RECEIVED.**  Every round in this family owes a SENTINEL-SEEDED READ ROW: seed the destination register, perform the declined read, and assert it reads back 0 -- a correct decline delivers zero, `return 0` faults and the register keeps its seed.  *That is the row `#497` was missing, and the measuring seat reached the same repair independently while this adjudication was being written.*  **TWO LATCH BITS, NOT ONE, and the precedent is MEASURED rather than stylistic.**  The arm prints two distinct messages -- `unimplemented write to address` and `unimplemented read from address`.  `#490` built the one-bit form as `M3shared` and **it ESCAPED**: a session declining at both sites sees the second fall silent because the first already latched.  `#279` states the rule outright -- *one flag per DISTINCT MESSAGE and never one shared flag*.  A single bit here means the first stray READ silences the first stray WRITE's message forever, and vice versa.  **THE DE-ESCALATION ACCEPT CLAUSE IS N/A, WITH ITS REASON RECORDED -- not unrun.**  The clause enumerates predicates that used to gate death and pins each accept side.  This arm **has no predicate**: it is the fall-through when none of the six window conditions match, so there is no adjacent modelled value because **nothing legal reaches it**.  The enumeration is the empty set.  *Adding a row to test the WINDOW predicates instead would be the addition licence's 'cannot hurt' wearing detector clothes -- and it is unnecessary, because `#497`'s own accept rows already prove those addresses reach their own arms rather than this one.  That is inherited coverage, not an uncovered hole.*  **WHAT IT OWES INSTEAD IS A CONTRACT SENTENCE (clause (e)):** this fix makes every unmatched address a silent zero-decline, so a future window-boundary regression that drops a legal address here no longer announces -- and that class is pinned by `#497`'s window accept rows, not by this arm.  *** AND A CONTRACT LIMIT WORTH KNOWING BEFORE ROWS ARE WRITTEN: the WRITE arm's `return 0` mutant is NOT distinguishable by a value-only cold-debugger probe. ***  A declined write and a faulting write both leave no readback.  The READ arm is killable by the seeded-read row; the write arm needs exception-state observation, which is a heavier probe shape than `#497` used.  **State it as a limit rather than shipping a completeness claim the rows cannot support.**  *** THE 32-BYTE GAP IS NOT DEAD SPACE.  IT IS `CRIME_RE_CLIP_IDS`, A SEVENTH REAL HARDWARE WINDOW. ***  `crmfbreg.h` defines `CRIME_RE_CLIP_IDS 0x16e0` between `CRIME_RE_TEX 0x1600` and `CRIME_RE_LINEAR_A 0x1700`, and `struct sgi_re_data` carries `// todo: clip_ids registers.` exactly where they would go.  The codex seat reached the same conclusion from the CRIME spec and put it best: **\"six real windows\" should read \"six MODELED windows\"** -- CLIP_IDS is real hardware with no dispatch and no state.  **The 2080-address count is UNCHANGED** (CLIP_IDS still falls to the kill) **but the characterisation splits, and the decline message must follow it:** 32 bytes are a documented, unmodelled register block; 2048 are genuinely unmapped.  A single \"unimplemented address\" diagnostic is accurate for the 2048 and WRONG for the 32.  *The brief asked whether `CRIME_RE_TEX + 0xe0` was a bug or the gap was real; both options were wrong, and a seat answering as posed would have had to pick the wrong one of two.  Three local facts agree the bound is deliberate: the next defined constant, the struct's own todo comment, and `re_tex[112]` x 2 bytes = 0xe0 exactly.*  *** THE fable5 SEAT'S EQUIVALENCE CLAIM IS REFUTED, AND THE REFUTATION CHANGES A DISPOSITION RATHER THAN SCORING A POINT. ***  It reported that an early `return 1` after the announce is OBSERVATIONALLY EQUIVALENT because *\"`memory_rw.c`'s #95 block memsets `data` to 0 on EVERY device read before the handler runs\"*, and advised disposing of that mutant as EQUIVALENT rather than chasing it.  **Checked both halves and both are false.**  The device handler is called at `memory_rw.c` `res = mem->devices[i].f(cpu, mem, paddr, data, len, ...)` and NOTHING zeroes `data` before it -- the three `memset(data, 0, len)` sites in that file are all on NON-DEVICE paths (the MIPS Sprite area, the beyond-physical_max arm, and the `memblock == NULL` unmapped-RAM arm), each followed by a `goto do_return_ok`.  And the MIPS caller's buffer is `uint8_t data[LS_SIZE];`, an **UNINITIALISED STACK ARRAY** with no memset anywhere in that file.  **SO THE MUTANT IS NOT EQUIVALENT -- BUT THE CORRECT DISPOSITION IS NOT 'DETECTABLE' EITHER, AND THAT NUANCE IS THE POINT.**  Skipping the tail leaves the guest whatever was on the stack, which is neither 0 nor any fixed value: the observable is REAL but NONDETERMINISTIC.  A row asserting a specific sentinel would be FLAKY, not sound.  *Filing it as EQUIVALENT would have retired a live mutant on a false mechanism; filing it as ordinary coverage debt would invite a flaky row.  It is neither, and the round must say so.*  **The seat's own remedy for the sibling `return 0` case is the one that works here too: seed the destination register with a sentinel and prove execution continued past the load** -- which pins the mutant without depending on stack contents.  *Everything else in that answer verified: `dev_unreadable.c` IS `return 0;` and `machine_sgi.c` installs it TEN times, so the FAULT member is real and in use on this machine.*  *** THE PASS-1 PANEL RESOLVED THE ROUND'S ONLY DISAGREEMENT BY WARRANT, NOT BY VOTE. ***  grok said ONE latch bit, deepseek said TWO.  I grepped first and found ZERO device files in the tree splitting a latch by direction -- which looked like precedent for grok.  **fable5 showed the framing was wrong: the house latch is per-MESSAGE-SITE, and this arm has TWO message texts (the write arm prints the data value, the read arm does not).  So two bits is not a new rule, it is the existing rule counted correctly** -- and my grep found nothing because I searched for the wrong property.  Two further warrants: a declined READ fabricates data the guest acts on while a declined write is a no-op, and one shared bit order-couples the two message rows so 'read message exactly once' becomes unpinnable.  *** WITNESSED AT RUNG 3, 32/32.  The row's witness field said `none`. ***  **AND ONE ROW FAILED FOR A REASON WORTH KEEPING: `lw` from 0x16ff SURVIVED** -- the log shows `exception ADEL`, because an unaligned load is refused by the CPU before `memory_rw` is reached.  Re-cut to 0x16fc it kills.  *The row was wrong, not the code, and the seat said so rather than adjusting the claim to fit.*  **THE ESCAPES, ranked by how little they cost:**  (1) *** A WINDOW-BOUND SHRINK (`+ 0x80` -> `+ 0x40`) SCORED A TOTAL ESCAPE, 9/9, AGAINST THE FIRST DETECTOR -- because the accept row wrote to the window's BASE, where a move of the FAR bound is invisible by construction. ***  That is clause (f)'s anchor rule arriving on the accept side, and the repair is one word: **an accept row must use the window's LAST slot.**  (2) a latch-bit collision reusing `SGIRE_W_TEX_LEN` scores 13/14, caught only by a row that touches another latch site first.  (3) `return 0` escapes both \"host survives\" and \"message once\" -- and the seat measured the discriminator side by side: with `v0` pre-set to **0** the row PASSES the mutant; pre-set to **0xa5a5a5a5** it FAILS, because a DBE leaves the register untouched.  The log is decisive (`reading from device 'sgi_re' ... failed` / `exception DBE`).  **So the brief's stated pin \"the declined read returns 0\" is MEASURED VACUOUS unless the sentinel is non-zero.**  **A REFINEMENT TO THE SIX-MEMBER OPTION SET, and it is not a quibble:** the set collapses THREE DISTINCT ANNOUNCE DISCIPLINES living in this one file -- unbounded-and-always-visible (`fatal()`, in sgi_de and sgi_mte), **unbounded but VERBOSITY-GATED (`debug()`, in sgi_de_status, invisible at default verbosity)**, and latched-once.  Folding the second into \"already declines\" hides exactly the flood question the round has to answer.  *** ROW COVERAGE, STATED RATHER THAN GLOSSED: ONLY 10 OF 14 ROWS EVER FIRED, AND THREE OF THE FOUR SILENT ONES ARE AN UNDISCHARGED OBLIGATION. ***  `accept tlba`, `accept tlbb` and `accept tlbc` never fired because **no shrink mutant was built for the three TLB windows**.  By the accept-row failability rule an accept row is discharged by a MEASURED KILL, not by existing -- so these three are NOT discharged, and the round owes three more mutants (about 1.5 s per build).  **AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME MUTANT SHAPE AS THE FOUR ALREADY BUILT.**  TLB_A and TLB_B are bounded by *the next window's constant* while the other four use *base + size*, so shrinking a TLB bound perturbs TWO arms at once -- it moves a boundary two windows share, rather than opening a gap.  A mutant written on the base+size pattern would not exercise them.  The fourth silent row, `read0 VACUOUS`, is **deliberately** never red: it is the control that DEMONSTRATES the brief's stated pin is vacuous.  Same load, `v0` pre-set to 0 instead of 0xa5a5a5a5, and it PASSES on the mutant where the real row fails.  *A row that never fires because it is a negative control and a row that never fires because nobody built its mutant must not be counted alike, which is why they are separated here.*  **DISPOSITION: this is L3, an undischarged obligation, not coverage debt** -- the three rows are the sole carriers of the de-escalation accept side for their windows, and the round's claim to have shipped accept rows is false about them until the mutants exist.  It is owed BEFORE the fix ships, not filed after.  *** RETRACTED.  MY REFUTATION OF fable5's EQUIVALENCE CLAIM WAS WRONG, AND I READ THE PRISTINE 2021 UPSTREAM COPY OF `memory_rw.c` TO PRODUCE IT.  fable5 WAS RIGHT. ***  I wrote that *\"nothing zeroes `data` before the handler call -- the three `memset(data, 0, len)` sites are all on NON-DEVICE paths\"*, and shipped it in `a115a4a`.  **The fork's `src/cpus/memory_rw.c` has SIX such sites, and one of them is exactly what fable5 described**, inside the device-found branch, before `paddr -= baseaddr` and before the dispatch:  `/*  #95: zero the caller's buffer up front on a read so a device handler that fills only part of it ... can never hand back uninitialised host memory to the guest.  */  if (writeflag == MEM_READ) memset(data, 0, len);`  *** HOW IT HAPPENED, AND IT IS THE HAZARD THIS PROJECT DOCUMENTS IN CAPITALS FOR SEATS -- IT CAUGHT THE MAIN LOOP INSTEAD. ***  There are two `memory_rw.c` under the project root: the fork at **794 lines** (`GXEMUL-SEC`, `est`, `build`, `build-asan`, md5 3d47dd66) and a PRISTINE 2021 UPSTREAM copy at **530 lines** (`./src/cpus/`, md5 75fbe149, plus two archive copies).  My grep ran as `src/cpus/memory_rw.c` while the cwd had drifted to the PROJECT ROOT, so it resolved to the upstream file.  **Every line number I reported -- 161, 222, 425, 437, 486 -- is below 530, and the fork's are 215/267/492/671/683/732.  The numbers themselves were the tell and I did not read them as one.**  **THE CONSEQUENTIAL ERROR, not the embarrassing one:** I dispositioned the early-`return 1` mutant as *\"REAL but NONDETERMINISTIC -- a row asserting a sentinel would be FLAKY\"*.  With #95 in place the guest gets a DETERMINISTIC ZERO, so on the value channel that mutant is **EQUIVALENT**, and a sentinel row is not flaky -- it is simply BLIND to it.  The opus5 seat measured this with two builds differing only in #95's fill byte: filling 0x00 gives `lbu = 0x0`, filling 0xa5 gives `lbu = 0xa5`, tracking byte-for-byte.  *So the disposition inverts: not coverage debt on a flaky channel, but an EQUIVALENT mutant that the mutant-that-will-not-die rule says to dispose of as a RESULT.  In `sgi_de` it is not equivalent overall, because that tail also DRAWS and `dec_switch` separates them -- but in `sgi_re`, whose tail is bare, opus5 expects true equivalence.*  **AND I TOLD THE OWNER I HAD REFUTED A SEAT.**  The correction is recorded here, in the carrier, and reported back -- because a wrong refutation that stands is worse than the original error: it teaches the next round to distrust the seat that was right.  *** L4 -- THE REASON THIS ROW GIVES FOR ITS OWN GAP IS WRONG FOR ONE OF ITS THREE MEMBERS, found 2026-08-31 by #499's PRE-TABLE block (sibling parity) before any mutant was built. ***  The note explains all three missing kills with *the TLB windows are bounded by the NEXT window's constant, not base+size*. So there is no slot from which both bounds are observable, and a single accept row pins exactly the bound it is near.  **THE REPAIR COSTS NO EXTRA SESSIONS AND IS STRICTLY BETTER THAN TWO ROWS:** the store's own `debug()` already prints the destination -- `[ d->re_linear_b[30] = 0x... ]`.  Assert **the array NAME and the INDEX** rather than merely that the catch-all was missed.  That converts each relative row into an ANCHORED row in clause (f)'s sense, pins BOTH bounds from one access, and additionally catches TRANSLATIONS -- which no base-or-top accept row can see at all.  Two rows (base AND top) is the fallback where no such channel exists, and it is weaker: it pins two bounds and still cannot see a re-map that keeps both.  **Because M5 weakens a predicate the fix DEPENDS ON and dominates the edited catch-all, it is clause (d) and therefore L2 -- repaired IN-ROUND, not filed as coverage debt.**  The carrier has been corrected in place; the detector repair ships with the fix.  *This is the freshly-minted-exemplar rule firing on itself: a rule minted from ONE instance carried that instance's shape as if it were the class, and the first round to APPLY it measured the general claim false within hours.  The instruction survived; the universal clause did not.*  *** LATCH-BIT DECONFLICTION BETWEEN TWO ROUNDS IN FLIGHT, caught 2026-08-31 BEFORE either shipped rather than measured afterwards. ***  `dev_sgi_re.c`'s `SGIRE_W_*` enum uses bits **0-6** today (`TLBA/TLBB/TLBC/TEX/LINA/LINB` 0-5, `DE_LEN` 6).  **`#499` takes `1 << 7` (`SGIRE_W_UNMAP_WR`) and `1 << 8` (`SGIRE_W_UNMAP_RD`)**, per its measured `mkvariant499.py` fix.  **`#500` was designed for `SGIRE_W_PRIMOP = 1 << 7` and MUST TAKE `1 << 9` instead.**  *A duplicate enumerator is legal C and silent, and both devices cast the same `struct sgi_re_data *`, so a collision makes one arm's decline permanently suppressible by unrelated traffic to the other -- measured as a 14/14 total escape when it happened between `#498` and `#499`'s generator.*  *** THE PORT SHIPPED AND IT CAUGHT THREE MUTANTS THE ARTIFACT DETECTOR LET THROUGH -- which is the L2 repair working, measured on the artifact that will actually run rather than inherited from the one that will not.  `regress/sgi_re_unmapped_probe.py`, 28 rows, self-contained, artifacts under killtable_20260831/port/. ***  **Re-measured on the SHIPPED probe:** `m4_bitcollide` **14/14 ESCAPED -> 27/28 KILLED**; `m5_lina_near` **14/14 -> 25/28** (three rows); the duplicate-1<<6 variant **14/14 -> 27/28**. Two word stores, no host timer. **Disarm is also synchronous**: a cmd write of 0x28 (`SCC_RESET_TX_IP`) -> `sio_tx_pending = 0` -> `INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->sio_irq)` -> bit 30 cleared -> `reassert_interrupts` — which, under an ASSERT-only NULL check, is a DEASSERT of NULL: the disarm doubles as the M_NULLCHECK row. Session-order caveat: bit 30 stays pending machine-wide until disarmed, so place the arming after W1 or disarm at once, or the pre-fix session dies on an arming store instead of a SOFT store.
.\regress\artifacts\lunaabsentcpu\fable_p1.md:45:**F8. The (a) equivalence class, and what "a NULL check guards the symptom" can and cannot mean.** (a) `cpu < d->ncpus`, (a') grok's `if (d->cpu_irq[cpu].interrupt_assert == NULL) continue;`, and (a'') a BOTH-calls NULL guard are observationally EQUIVALENT on every reachable configuration: ncpus in {1..4} (DEVINIT's `ncpus > 4` exit at the `LUNA 88K can't have more than 4 CPUs` printf is reached FIRST — `device_add(machine, "luna88k")` precedes `machine_luna88k.c`'s own check), connect <=> populated, and a failed connect cannot be silent (`interrupt_handler_lookup` prints and `abort()`s on an unresolvable name). So the ruling's rejection of the NULL check is WARRANT-based (a guard that hides a future wiring bug), not measurable — glm is right, and the PRETABLE must record the both-calls guard as an EQUIVALENT mutant, not an escape. The ASSERT-only guard (M_NULLCHECK) is NOT equivalent: it leaves `irqActive[1] = 1` and dies on the next else-branch DEASSERT.
.\regress\artifacts\lunaabsentcpu\fable_p1.md:47:**F9. src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:91:	    EIGHT bytes inside the device's own 8192-byte registration ending the

src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:93:	    edit, on unmodified `-E mvme88k -e mvme187':
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:94:	        artifacts/mk48nvramkill/opus_mk48_nvram_witness_20260910.py
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:95:	        artifacts/mk48nvramkill/opus_witness_run_20260910.txt  -- 15/47,
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:96:	            all 15 controls green and all 32 kill rows red.
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:97:	    THE POPULATION IS SIXTEEN CELLS, NOT EIGHT: every offset kills in BOTH
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:98:	    directions, ld.bu and st.b alike, and all sixteen were driven.  The kill
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:99:	    is WIDTH-INDEPENDENT -- `len' never enters the guard, so ld / ld.h /
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:100:	    ld.bu all die at the same start offset and there is no width axis here.
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:101:

src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:103:	    accepting them rather than declining.  mk48txxreg.h says the first bank
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:104:	    of eight registers at (nvramsz - 16) -- exactly 0x1ff0 -- is "available

src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:106:	    IAMIN, IAHOUR, IADAY, IINTR, IWDOG.  They are the MK48T59's alarm and
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:107:	    watchdog bank; this file implements the MK48T08 alone, where the region
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:108:	    is plain non-volatile RAM.  Storage already existed -- `reg' is
src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:109:	    MK48TXX_LEN bytes -- so this is a BOUNDARY CORRECTION, not new policy,

src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:111:

1:#!/usr/bin/env python3
2:"""mk48_nvram_probe.py -- the #521 DETECTOR for dev_mk48txx.c (mk48nvramkill).
3:
4:PROVENANCE.  This is the round's rung-3 WITNESS, shipped as its detector with
5:exactly ONE row inverted (see "msg silent" below).  The witness as run is
6:preserved unmodified at
7:    regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/opus_mk48_nvram_witness_20260910.py
8:and scored 15/47 on the committed build and 46/47 after the fix; the 46th was
9:that one row, which by construction cannot be green on a fixed build.  This
10:file therefore scores 47/47 post-fix and 14/47 pre-fix.
11:
12:
13:DEFECT.  DEVICE_ACCESS(mk48txx) accepts everything outside
14:[MK48T08_CLKOFF, MK48T08_CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ISEC) = [0x1ff0, 0x1ff9) via an early
15:`goto ret'.  The switch it then reaches holds exactly ONE case,
16:MK48T08_CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ICSR = 0x1ff8.  The other EIGHT offsets 0x1ff0..0x1ff7
17:fall to `default:' -> fatal() -> exit(1), in BOTH directions.
18:
19:CONSTANTS VERIFIED IN-TREE (src/include/thirdparty/mk48txxreg.h):
20:    MK48T08_CLKSZ  8192 (=MK48TXX_LEN, the registration length)
21:    MK48T08_CLKOFF 0x1ff0      MK48TXX_ICSR 8      MK48TXX_ISEC 9
22:Attach: src/machines/machine_mvme88k.c, MVME187 subtype, "mk48txx addr=0xfffc0000".
23:So the killing guest physical addresses are 0xfffc1ff0 .. 0xfffc1ff7.
24:
25:REACHABILITY -- WHY THE CENSUS IS ALL EIGHT, NOT THE PCC2 SUBSET.  dev_pcc2.c's
26:width kills were half-unreachable because a non-byte m88k access is alignment-checked
27:before memory_rw.  Here the killing accesses are BYTE accesses: cpu_m88k_instr.c
28:decodes op26 0x03 as ld.bu (opsize 0) and 0x0b as st.b (opsize 0), and
29:cpu_m88k_instr_loadstore.c guards its alignment check with `#ifndef LS_1', so a
30:size-1 access is never alignment-checked.  Every one of the eight offsets is
31:guest-reachable in BOTH directions: 16 killing accesses, and the full census is free.
32:
33:RUNG 3: real MC88100 instructions executed by the CPU through real address decode
34:and real memory_rw, on the UNMODIFIED in-tree `-E mvme88k -e mvme187'.  No machine
35:description edited, no device_add added -- mvme187 already attaches this device.
36:
37:THE ORACLE, and why it is not a timeout and not a grep.  The symptom is PROCESS
38:DEATH.  Four independent discriminators, and a kill row is scored on the FIRST TWO
39:jointly:
40:  (1) REAPED WAIT STATUS.  subprocess returncode 1 == exit(1); 0 == the debugger's
41:      own `quit'.  A HANG is a distinct third state (TimeoutExpired), so "timed out"
42:      can never be read as "killed".
43:  (2) POST-ACCESS OBSERVABLE.  Every session is st r5,r3,0 ; <ACCESS> ; ld r4,r3,0
44:      and ends `print r4'.  If the guest ran on, r4 == 0x5a5a5a5a; if the host died,
45:      the third step and the print never happen.  This separates DEATH from a
46:      fatal() that merely COMPLAINS AND RETURNS -- which this fork has many of.
47:  (3) ATTRIBUTION BY MESSAGE.  The message names the offset, so the death is tied to
48:      the access rather than to the session.  Corroboration, never proof on its own.
49:  (4) ATTRIBUTION BY SIBLING.  Otherwise-identical sessions at 0x1fef (below the
50:      window), 0x1ff8 (the implemented case) and 0x1ff9 (first accepted clock reg)
51:      must run to completion.
52:
53:CONTROLS, clause (ii) of the witness ladder:
54:  live      a byte written to NVRAM offset 0x100 round-trips through the same decode
55:  nvram0    a never-written neighbour reads 0x00 -- the array is zero-filled, so a
56:            nonzero clock byte cannot be zero-fill or a stuck bus
57:  devsig *  FOUR clock registers (0x1ffc..0x1fff) are compared against the HOST's own
58:            gmtime, each with its own encoding: year/mon/mday are BCD, wday is
59:            tm_wday+1 RAW.  A four-way match with two different encodings could only
60:            come from mk48txx_update_regs().  RAM answers 0; a fallback answers 0.
61:  shape     the identical session with a RAM load in the access slot exits 0.
62:
63:TEST-FIRST SHAPE: every `want' below is the FIXED behaviour.  On the committed
64:pre-fix build the 32 kill rows red; after a fix the same script goes green.
65:
66:Sentinel: MK48_WITNESS_DONE  (kept: the driver greps for it)
67:"""

72:import time

75:    sys.stderr.write("usage: mk48_nvram_witness.py <gxemul> [label]\n")
76:    sys.exit(2)

83:CODE = 0x00010000
84:SCRATCH = 0x00020000
85:MK48 = 0xfffc0000                 # machine_mvme88k.c, MVME187
86:CLKOFF = 0x1ff0
87:ICSR = 8

91:def enc(op26, rd, rs1, imm):
92:    return (op26 << 26) | (rd << 21) | (rs1 << 16) | (imm & 0xffff)
93:
94:
95:def LD_W(rd, rs1, i):
96:    return enc(0x05, rd, rs1, i)          # ld    (word)
97:
98:
99:def LD_BU(rd, rs1, i):
100:    return enc(0x03, rd, rs1, i)          # ld.bu (byte, unsigned, never alignment-checked)
101:
102:
103:def ST_W(rd, rs1, i):
104:    return enc(0x09, rd, rs1, i)          # st    (word)
105:
106:
107:def ST_B(rd, rs1, i):
108:    return enc(0x0b, rd, rs1, i)          # st.b  (byte,           never alignment-checked)

111:BR_SELF = 0xc0000000
112:SENT = 0xdeadbeef
113:SETUP = ["r2=0x%x" % MK48, "r3=0x%x" % SCRATCH, "r5=0x5a5a5a5a",
114:         "r4=0x%x" % SENT, "r6=0x%x" % SENT, "r7=0x%x" % SENT, "r8=0x%x" % SENT]

120:def row(name, got, want, note=""):
121:    assert name not in [n for n, _ in rows], "DUPLICATE ROW NAME %r" % name
122:    assert len(name) <= 15, name
123:    ok = (got == want)
124:    rows.append((name, ok))
125:    print("%-16s %-22s %-22s %-54s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note,
126:                                          "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
127:    return ok

129:
130:def program(insns, at=CODE):
131:    words = list(insns) + [BR_SELF, 0]
132:    cmds = ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * i, w) for i, w in enumerate(words)]
133:    return cmds + ["pc=0x%x" % at, "step %d" % len(insns)]
134:
135:
136:def session(cmds, timeout=60, subtype="mvme187"):
137:    """-> (transcript, how) where how is exit0 / exit1 / exitN / sigN / TIMEOUT."""
138:    argv = [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "mvme88k", "-e", subtype, "-M", "64",
139:            "0x10000:" + DUMMY]
140:    inp = ("\n".join(list(cmds) + ["quit"]) + "\n").encode("latin-1")
141:    try:
142:        p = subprocess.run(argv, input=inp, stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
143:                           stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, timeout=timeout)
144:    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as e:
145:        out = (e.output or b"").decode("latin-1", "replace").replace("\r", "")
146:        return out, "TIMEOUT"
147:    rc = p.returncode
148:    how = ("sig%d" % -rc) if rc < 0 else ("exit%d" % rc)
149:    return p.stdout.decode("latin-1", "replace").replace("\r", ""), how
150:
151:
152:def printed(t, reg):
153:    """the bare 0x... line the debugger emits after `print rN', skipping chatter."""
154:    m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\b" % reg, t)
155:    if not m:
156:        return "absent"
157:    for line in t[m.end():].split("\n")[1:]:
158:        s = line.strip()
159:        if not s or s.startswith("["):
160:            continue
161:        mm = re.match(r"^(0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)$", s)
162:        return ("0x%x" % int(mm.group(1), 16)) if mm else "absent"
163:    return "absent"

166:def access_session(access_word):
167:    """The access under test always sits between a scratch STORE and a scratch LOAD,
168:       so a survivor has a positive observable and a corpse has none."""
169:    return session(SETUP + program([ST_W(5, 3, 0), access_word, LD_W(4, 3, 0)])
170:                   + ["print r4"])

173:def bcd(x):
174:    return ((x // 10) << 4) + (x % 10)

200:# DEVICE SIGNATURE: four clock regs vs the HOST clock, in two different encodings.
201:#
202:# *** C's struct tm AND PYTHON'S struct_time DISAGREE ON TWO OF THESE FOUR FIELDS,
203:# and the first run of this probe got tm_mon wrong and reported the DEVICE as
204:# defective when the device was right. ***
205:#     tm_mon   C: 0..11   Python: 1..12   -> device BCD(tm_mon+1) == bcd(py tm_mon)
206:#     tm_wday  C: 0=Sun   Python: 0=Mon   -> C wday == (py tm_wday + 1) % 7
207:#     tm_year  C: yr-1900 Python: full    -> equal mod 100 for 2000..2099 only
208:#     tm_mday  same in both.
209:g = time.gmtime()
210:t, how = session(SETUP + program([LD_BU(4, 2, CLKOFF + 15), LD_BU(6, 2, CLKOFF + 14),
211:                                  LD_BU(7, 2, CLKOFF + 13), LD_BU(8, 2, CLKOFF + 12)])

216:    "0x1ffe = BCD(host UTC month); py tm_mon is ALREADY 1-based")

218:    "0x1ffd = BCD(host UTC day of month)")
219:row("devsig wday", printed(t, "r8"), "0x%x" % (((g.tm_wday + 1) % 7) + 1),
220:    "0x1ffc = tm_wday+1 RAW, NOT BCD -- a second encoding, same block")

223:                 + ["print r4"])

227:# sibling-offset attribution controls
228:for lbl, off, wr in (("ok rd 1fef", 0x1fef, 0), ("ok wr 1fef", 0x1fef, 1),
229:                     ("ok rd 1ff8", 0x1ff8, 0), ("ok wr 1ff8", 0x1ff8, 1),
230:                     ("ok rd 1ff9", 0x1ff9, 0), ("ok wr 1ff9", 0x1ff9, 1)):

234:        "0x%04x %s -- accepted arm, must run to completion"
235:        % (off, "wr" if wr else "rd"))

237:#  ------------------------------------------------------------- CSR RISING EDGE
238:#
239:#  *** THIS ROW EXISTS BECAUSE THE FIRST KILL TABLE FOUND A FLOOR BREACH: M_ICSR
240:#  ESCAPED 47/47. ***  That mutant moves the accept block's UPPER bound to
241:#  CLKOFF+ICSR, making the condition `addr < 0x1ff8 || addr >= 0x1ff8' a TAUTOLOGY --
242:#  every address is accepted and the switch is NEVER REACHED, so 0x1ff8 is served as
243:#  plain RAM and the control register stops doing its work.
244:#
245:#  The rows `ok rd 1ff8' / `ok wr 1ff8' could not see it, and the reason is the one
246:#  this project keeps re-learning: THEY ARE SURVIVE-ONLY AT THAT OFFSET.  They assert
247:#  the access ran to completion, which is true on the RAM path too.  A survival oracle
248:#  cannot distinguish two arms that both survive.
249:#
250:#  THE CHANNEL THAT DOES VARY is the ICSR case's SIDE EFFECT.  Writing the READ bit
251:#  0 -> 1 calls mk48txx_update_regs(), refreshing the clock registers from the host.
252:#  So: clear ICSR, poison a clock slot, drive the rising edge, read the slot back.
253:#  Fixed build -> the poison is gone, replaced by the host value.  M_ICSR -> the
254:#  poison survives, because no update ever ran.
255:#
256:#  r5's low byte is 0x5a, and 0x5a & MK48TXX_CSR_READ (0x40) is 0x40, so ST_B of r5
257:#  BOTH poisons a slot and drives the edge -- no new instruction encoding is needed.
258:#  0x5a is a safe poison: BCD day-of-month never exceeds 0x31.
259:t, how = session(SETUP + program([ST_B(0, 2, CLKOFF + 8),      # ICSR = 0, READ bit clear
260:                                  ST_B(5, 2, CLKOFF + 13),     # poison the mday slot
261:                                  ST_B(5, 2, CLKOFF + 8),      # READ bit 0 -> 1
262:                                  LD_BU(4, 2, CLKOFF + 13)])
263:                 + ["print r4"])
264:row("csr rise", printed(t, "r4"), "0x%x" % bcd(g.tm_mday),
265:    "ICSR read-bit 0->1 must REFRESH the clock regs (kills M_ICSR)")
266:
267:# ------------------------------------------------------------------ the kills
268:#  full census: eight offsets x two directions, all byte-width, all guest-reachable
269:for wr in (0, 1):
270:    for off in range(CLKOFF, CLKOFF + ICSR):          # 0x1ff0 .. 0x1ff7
271:        w = ST_B(5, 2, off) if wr else LD_BU(4, 2, off)
272:        t, how = access_session(w)
273:        tag = "%s %04x" % ("wr" if wr else "rd", off)
274:        want_msg = ("[ mk48txx: unimplemented %s offset 0x%x"
275:                    % ("WRITE to" if wr else "READ from", off))
276:        if want_msg in t:
277:            msgs.append(tag)
278:        row(tag, how, "exit0",
279:            "%s of 0x%04x must not end the host process"
280:            % ("st.b" if wr else "ld.bu", off))
281:        row(tag + " obs", printed(t, "r4") if how == "exit0" else "died",
282:            "0x5a5a5a5a",
283:            "...and the guest runs on -- kills a death however it is spelled")

290:#  old boundary brings all sixteen messages back.  CHANNEL: the count itself,

regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:33:   properly. `MK48TXX_ICSR` is 8 and `MK48TXX_ISEC` is 9 through `MK48TXX_IYEAR` at 15, so indices 0..7
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:34:   of the clock page are unaccounted for by any `MK48TXX_I*` define. **What are they on an MK48T08?**
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:35:   Options I can see, and I want you to say which the header and the sibling drivers support: they are
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:36:   ordinary NVRAM; they are a second bank; they are the chip's *other* model's registers (the file says
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:37:   *"Only the MK48T08 is implemented so far"*); or the accept block's bound should have been
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:38:   `CLKOFF + ICSR` rather than `CLKOFF + ISEC`. **Note the current bound has a second consequence worth
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:39:   checking: it hands the REAL time-of-day registers (`ISEC..IYEAR`) to the RAM path** -- is that
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:40:   correct, harmless, or a separate defect?
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:41:2. **WHICH OPTION-SET MEMBER?** This project's menu is {delete, silent decline, decline + latched
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:42:   announce, accept, fault, backed accept, implement}. **Grep the fork for how sibling timekeeper and
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:43:   NVRAM devices handle an offset they do not model** -- `dev_mc146818.c` and `dev_rs5c313.c` are the
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:44:   obvious neighbours and `dev_rs5c313.c` already cites `mk48txxreg.h` in a comment. The tree's own
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:45:   precedent is worth more than an argument. **And check the member is AVAILABLE**: a bus fault needs the
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:46:   memory layer to deliver one, which this tree does for only two CPU families, and this device is
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:47:   attached to an m88k machine.

regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:57:   them is silently discarded, silently honoured, or honoured and then overwritten**, because that

regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:14:`src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c` models the MK48T08 clock/NVRAM chip. Grep anchor for the site:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:15:`default:if (writeflag == MEM_READ)`.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:16:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:17:The device is registered for `MK48TXX_LEN` = `MK48T08_CLKSZ` = **8192** bytes, so `relative_addr` spans
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:18:`[0, 0x2000)`. An early accept block passes everything OUTSIDE
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:19:`[MK48T08_CLKOFF, MK48T08_CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ISEC)` straight to the RAM path with a `goto ret`. With
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:20:`MK48T08_CLKOFF` = `0x1ff0` and `MK48TXX_ISEC` = `9`, the `switch` is reached for exactly the nine offsets
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:21:`0x1ff0..0x1ff8` -- and it holds exactly ONE case, `MK48T08_CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ICSR` = `0x1ff8`.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:22:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:23:**So the eight offsets `0x1ff0` through `0x1ff7` fall to `default:` and call `exit(1)`.**
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:24:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:25:`src/machines/machine_mvme88k.c` attaches the device unconditionally at **`0xfffc0000`**, in the same
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:26:block that attaches the PCC2 SCSI controller. So the eight fatal offsets should be at guest physical
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:27:`0xfffc1ff0` .. `0xfffc1ff7`.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:28:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:29:**Please do not simply ratify this.** Check the constants, the registration length and the attach address
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:30:yourself; if any of them is different from what I have written, the whole round changes.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:31:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:32:## WHAT I AM ASKING FOR
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:33:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:34:**A RUNG-3 WITNESS: a real guest access through real address decode and real `memory_rw`, on an
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:35:UNMODIFIED in-tree machine description.** This project's ladder is explicit that a `#include`/direct-call
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:36:harness is NOT a reproduction, and that adding a machine or a `device_add` in order to reach the site is
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:37:laundering rather than witnessing. `mvme88k` already attaches this device, so no such addition is needed.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:38:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:39:`regress/` holds many existing pty-driven probes you can copy the shape of -- `_scratchpad/gxprobe.py` is
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:40:the validated harness and the project's own rule is *don't hand-roll pty plumbing*. Several probes drive
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:41:the debugger with `step`; look at one and follow it.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:42:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:43:**A VALID witness needs all of these, and this project has been burned by each:**
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:44:1. **It shows the symptom on the COMMITTED build.** `C:\DocumentNoSnc\CC\GXEMUL\build\gxemul` is current
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:45:   and clean (source `cmp`s CLEAN against the repo, binary is newer than the source). There is no fix
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:46:   yet, so a green run means the witness is wrong, not that the defect is absent.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:47:2. **A LIVENESS control** -- a known value returns through the SAME decode. Offset `0x1ff9` and anything
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:48:   below `0x1ff0` take the RAM path, so a write-then-read round trip there should work and proves the
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:49:   device is mapped, responding, and reached at the address you think.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:50:3. **A DEVICE-SIGNATURE control** -- something proving the value came from THIS DEVICE rather than from
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:51:   RAM or a fallback. *The project's footbridge probe once returned `0x0` everywhere with its RAM control
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:52:   green, so one control is not enough.* The clock registers at `0x1ff9..0x1fff` are filled by
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:53:   `mk48txx_update_regs()` from the host clock and are a candidate signature; find your own if that does
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:54:   not work.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:55:4. **The machine description and dispatch UNMODIFIED.**
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:56:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:57:## THE THING THAT MAKES THIS ORACLE UNUSUAL, and I want your judgement on it
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:58:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:59:**The symptom is PROCESS DEATH, not a wrong value.** `exit(1)` ends the emulator, so the observable is
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:60:that the child terminates and prints `[ mk48txx: unimplemented READ from offset 0x1ff0 ]`. That is easy
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:61:to see and easy to get WRONG:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:62:* a probe that merely times out cannot distinguish a kill from a hang;
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:63:* a probe that greps for the message does not establish that the process DIED, only that it complained --
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:64:  and this fork has many `fatal()` calls that RETURN (`fatal()` has no early-out and does not exit);
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:65:* the emulator can exit for unrelated reasons, so the run needs to show the death is ATTRIBUTABLE to
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:66:  this access -- the natural control being an otherwise identical session that reads `0x1ff9` instead and
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:67:  runs to completion.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:68:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:69:**Please design the oracle to separate those, and say in your answer which discriminator you used.**
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_witness.md:70:

1283-> site**, and (c) fails on a null medium (a stale copy, a comment-only file — i.e. it carries
1284-> identity rows). Below the floor, the round's own claim to have "shipped a detector with the
1285-> fix" is a WRONG RECORD, which the stopping rule already fixes in-round.

1638-> #### AND THE OBVIOUS GENERALISATION IS UNBOUNDED, SO IT IS NOT THE ONE ADOPTED
1639->
1640-> The tempting sentence is *"every VALUE and every STATE in which a guard refuses needs a
1641-> decline-channel row."*  **That licenses unbounded row growth -- the `#392` shape** -- and the
1642-> adjudication that produced this line rejected it for exactly that reason. The distinction:
1643->
1644-> * **ROW DEMAND** -- *which* refusals need pinning -- is ALREADY bounded and is not touched:
1645:>   the floor clauses, the (d) DEPENDS-ON closure, one accept row per conditioned predicate,
1646->   one anchor row per position claim, one ask-again. **Rows are owed PER ENUMERATED MUTANT,
1647->   never per refusing state**; two states no enumerated mutant distinguishes collapse into one
1648->   row. That enumeration is diff-closed and finite.
1649-> * **ROW VALIDITY** -- what `#489` actually measured -- is the CHANNEL line above, and it is a
1650->   WRITE-TIME check on rows that already exist.
1651->
1652-> > **A FILTER CAN REJECT OR REPAIR A ROW; IT CAN NEVER DEMAND A NEW ONE -- SO IT CANNOT GROW
1653-> > WITHOUT BOUND.**  That is the stop condition, and it is structural rather than a cap: the
1654-> > generator side is untouched.
1655->
1656-> *Mechanising it -- a `# kills: <mutant> via <channel>` comment per row -- is FILED, not
1657-> adopted: it changes what every probe in the tree must carry, and that is an owner decision
1658-> rather than a round's.*
1659->

1661-> unreachable because it was approached from the mutant side -- *"what edit escapes?"*,
1662-> which is unbounded -- instead of the field side -- *"what states can these two fields be
1663-> in?"*, which is a grep. In `#462` that was three lines of output: `alloc_data` sets both
1664-> fields; two `free` sites clear only one.

1667-> from shapes the author already covered; measured yield says so. And do not raise the cap

 succeeded in 137ms:
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """mk48_nvram_probe.py -- the #521 DETECTOR for dev_mk48txx.c (mk48nvramkill).
3: 
4: PROVENANCE.  This is the round's rung-3 WITNESS, shipped as its detector with
5: exactly ONE row inverted (see "msg silent" below).  The witness as run is
6: preserved unmodified at
7:     regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/opus_mk48_nvram_witness_20260910.py
8: and scored 15/47 on the committed build and 46/47 after the fix; the 46th was
9: that one row, which by construction cannot be green on a fixed build.  This
10: file therefore scores 47/47 post-fix and 14/47 pre-fix.
11: 
12: 
13: DEFECT.  DEVICE_ACCESS(mk48txx) accepts everything outside
14: [MK48T08_CLKOFF, MK48T08_CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ISEC) = [0x1ff0, 0x1ff9) via an early
15: `goto ret'.  The switch it then reaches holds exactly ONE case,
16: MK48T08_CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ICSR = 0x1ff8.  The other EIGHT offsets 0x1ff0..0x1ff7
17: fall to `default:' -> fatal() -> exit(1), in BOTH directions.
18: 
19: CONSTANTS VERIFIED IN-TREE (src/include/thirdparty/mk48txxreg.h):
20:     MK48T08_CLKSZ  8192 (=MK48TXX_LEN, the registration length)
21:     MK48T08_CLKOFF 0x1ff0      MK48TXX_ICSR 8      MK48TXX_ISEC 9
22: Attach: src/machines/machine_mvme88k.c, MVME187 subtype, "mk48txx addr=0xfffc0000".
23: So the killing guest physical addresses are 0xfffc1ff0 .. 0xfffc1ff7.
24: 
25: REACHABILITY -- WHY THE CENSUS IS ALL EIGHT, NOT THE PCC2 SUBSET.  dev_pcc2.c's
26: width kills were half-unreachable because a non-byte m88k access is alignment-checked
27: before memory_rw.  Here the killing accesses are BYTE accesses: cpu_m88k_instr.c
28: decodes op26 0x03 as ld.bu (opsize 0) and 0x0b as st.b (opsize 0), and
29: cpu_m88k_instr_loadstore.c guards its alignment check with `#ifndef LS_1', so a
30: size-1 access is never alignment-checked.  Every one of the eight offsets is
31: guest-reachable in BOTH directions: 16 killing accesses, and the full census is free.
32: 
33: RUNG 3: real MC88100 instructions executed by the CPU through real address decode
34: and real memory_rw, on the UNMODIFIED in-tree `-E mvme88k -e mvme187'.  No machine
35: description edited, no device_add added -- mvme187 already attaches this device.
36: 
37: THE ORACLE, and why it is not a timeout and not a grep.  The symptom is PROCESS
38: DEATH.  Four independent discriminators, and a kill row is scored on the FIRST TWO
39: jointly:
40:   (1) REAPED WAIT STATUS.  subprocess returncode 1 == exit(1); 0 == the debugger's
41:       own `quit'.  A HANG is a distinct third state (TimeoutExpired), so "timed out"
42:       can never be read as "killed".
43:   (2) POST-ACCESS OBSERVABLE.  Every session is st r5,r3,0 ; <ACCESS> ; ld r4,r3,0
44:       and ends `print r4'.  If the guest ran on, r4 == 0x5a5a5a5a; if the host died,
45:       the third step and the print never happen.  This separates DEATH from a
46:       fatal() that merely COMPLAINS AND RETURNS -- which this fork has many of.
47:   (3) ATTRIBUTION BY MESSAGE.  The message names the offset, so the death is tied to
48:       the access rather than to the session.  Corroboration, never proof on its own.
49:   (4) ATTRIBUTION BY SIBLING.  Otherwise-identical sessions at 0x1fef (below the
50:       window), 0x1ff8 (the implemented case) and 0x1ff9 (first accepted clock reg)
51:       must run to completion.
52: 
53: CONTROLS, clause (ii) of the witness ladder:
54:   live      a byte written to NVRAM offset 0x100 round-trips through the same decode
55:   nvram0    a never-written neighbour reads 0x00 -- the array is zero-filled, so a
56:             nonzero clock byte cannot be zero-fill or a stuck bus
57:   devsig *  FOUR clock registers (0x1ffc..0x1fff) are compared against the HOST's own
58:             gmtime, each with its own encoding: year/mon/mday are BCD, wday is
59:             tm_wday+1 RAW.  A four-way match with two different encodings could only
60:             come from mk48txx_update_regs().  RAM answers 0; a fallback answers 0.
61:   shape     the identical session with a RAM load in the access slot exits 0.
62: 
63: TEST-FIRST SHAPE: every `want' below is the FIXED behaviour.  On the committed
64: pre-fix build the 32 kill rows red; after a fix the same script goes green.
65: 
66: Sentinel: MK48_WITNESS_DONE  (kept: the driver greps for it)
67: """
68: 
69: import re
70: import subprocess
71: import sys
72: import time
73: 
74: if len(sys.argv) < 2:
75:     sys.stderr.write("usage: mk48_nvram_witness.py <gxemul> [label]\n")
76:     sys.exit(2)
77: 
78: BIN = sys.argv[1]
79: LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "local"
80: DUMMY = "/tmp/mk48_dummy.bin"
81: open(DUMMY, "wb").write(b"\x00\x00\x00\x00")
82: 
83: CODE = 0x00010000
84: SCRATCH = 0x00020000
85: MK48 = 0xfffc0000                 # machine_mvme88k.c, MVME187
86: CLKOFF = 0x1ff0
87: ICSR = 8
88: ISEC = 9
89: 
90: 
91: def enc(op26, rd, rs1, imm):
92:     return (op26 << 26) | (rd << 21) | (rs1 << 16) | (imm & 0xffff)
93: 
94: 
95: def LD_W(rd, rs1, i):
96:     return enc(0x05, rd, rs1, i)          # ld    (word)
97: 
98: 
99: def LD_BU(rd, rs1, i):
100:     return enc(0x03, rd, rs1, i)          # ld.bu (byte, unsigned, never alignment-checked)
101: 
102: 
103: def ST_W(rd, rs1, i):
104:     return enc(0x09, rd, rs1, i)          # st    (word)
105: 
106: 
107: def ST_B(rd, rs1, i):
108:     return enc(0x0b, rd, rs1, i)          # st.b  (byte,           never alignment-checked)
109: 
110: 
111: BR_SELF = 0xc0000000
112: SENT = 0xdeadbeef
113: SETUP = ["r2=0x%x" % MK48, "r3=0x%x" % SCRATCH, "r5=0x5a5a5a5a",
114:          "r4=0x%x" % SENT, "r6=0x%x" % SENT, "r7=0x%x" % SENT, "r8=0x%x" % SENT]
115: 
116: rows = []
117: msgs = []
118: 
119: 
120: def row(name, got, want, note=""):
121:     assert name not in [n for n, _ in rows], "DUPLICATE ROW NAME %r" % name
122:     assert len(name) <= 15, name
123:     ok = (got == want)
124:     rows.append((name, ok))
125:     print("%-16s %-22s %-22s %-54s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note,
126:                                           "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
127:     return ok
128: 
129: 
130: def program(insns, at=CODE):
131:     words = list(insns) + [BR_SELF, 0]
132:     cmds = ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * i, w) for i, w in enumerate(words)]
133:     return cmds + ["pc=0x%x" % at, "step %d" % len(insns)]
134: 
135: 
136: def session(cmds, timeout=60, subtype="mvme187"):
137:     """-> (transcript, how) where how is exit0 / exit1 / exitN / sigN / TIMEOUT."""
138:     argv = [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "mvme88k", "-e", subtype, "-M", "64",
139:             "0x10000:" + DUMMY]
140:     inp = ("\n".join(list(cmds) + ["quit"]) + "\n").encode("latin-1")
141:     try:
142:         p = subprocess.run(argv, input=inp, stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
143:                            stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, timeout=timeout)
144:     except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as e:
145:         out = (e.output or b"").decode("latin-1", "replace").replace("\r", "")
146:         return out, "TIMEOUT"
147:     rc = p.returncode
148:     how = ("sig%d" % -rc) if rc < 0 else ("exit%d" % rc)
149:     return p.stdout.decode("latin-1", "replace").replace("\r", ""), how
150: 
151: 
152: def printed(t, reg):
153:     """the bare 0x... line the debugger emits after `print rN', skipping chatter."""
154:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\b" % reg, t)
155:     if not m:
156:         return "absent"
157:     for line in t[m.end():].split("\n")[1:]:
158:         s = line.strip()
159:         if not s or s.startswith("["):
160:             continue
161:         mm = re.match(r"^(0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)$", s)
162:         return ("0x%x" % int(mm.group(1), 16)) if mm else "absent"
163:     return "absent"
164: 
165: 
166: def access_session(access_word):
167:     """The access under test always sits between a scratch STORE and a scratch LOAD,
168:        so a survivor has a positive observable and a corpse has none."""
169:     return session(SETUP + program([ST_W(5, 3, 0), access_word, LD_W(4, 3, 0)])
170:                    + ["print r4"])
171: 
172: 
173: def bcd(x):
174:     return ((x // 10) << 4) + (x % 10)
175: 
176: 
177: print("mk48nvramkill RUNG-3 WITNESS -- binary=%s label=%s" % (BIN, LABEL))
178: print("machine: -E mvme88k -e mvme187 -M 64 (UNMODIFIED); device at 0x%08x" % MK48)
179: print("%-16s %-22s %-22s %-54s %s" % ("row", "got", "want", "note", ""))
180: t0 = time.time()
181: 
182: # ------------------------------------------------------------------- controls
183: t, how = session(SETUP + program([ST_B(5, 2, 0x0100), LD_BU(4, 2, 0x0100),
184:                                   LD_BU(6, 2, 0x0101)])
185:                  + ["print r4", "print r6"])
186: row("live", printed(t, "r4"), "0x5a",
187:     "a byte round-trips through the SAME decode (NVRAM 0x100)")
188: row("nvram0", printed(t, "r6"), "0x0",
189:     "a never-written neighbour reads 0 -- the array is zero-filled")
190: 
191: # APPARATUS NEGATIVE CONTROL.  Prove the liveness oracle CAN go red, so a green
192: # `live' is evidence rather than a check that cannot fail.  pcc2_width_witness.py
193: # learned this by accident: `-e 187' is not a subtype, the machine never starts, and
194: # EVERY row went red including the pure-RAM control.  Here that state is asserted.
195: t, how = session(SETUP + program([ST_B(5, 2, 0x0100), LD_BU(4, 2, 0x0100)])
196:                  + ["print r4"], subtype="187")
197: row("neg apparatus", "%s/%s" % (how, printed(t, "r4")), "exit1/absent",
198:     "bogus subtype -> no machine -> `live' MUST fail; the oracle is failable")
199: 
200: # DEVICE SIGNATURE: four clock regs vs the HOST clock, in two different encodings.
201: #
202: # *** C's struct tm AND PYTHON'S struct_time DISAGREE ON TWO OF THESE FOUR FIELDS,
203: # and the first run of this probe got tm_mon wrong and reported the DEVICE as
204: # defective when the device was right. ***
205: #     tm_mon   C: 0..11   Python: 1..12   -> device BCD(tm_mon+1) == bcd(py tm_mon)
206: #     tm_wday  C: 0=Sun   Python: 0=Mon   -> C wday == (py tm_wday + 1) % 7
207: #     tm_year  C: yr-1900 Python: full    -> equal mod 100 for 2000..2099 only
208: #     tm_mday  same in both.
209: g = time.gmtime()
210: t, how = session(SETUP + program([LD_BU(4, 2, CLKOFF + 15), LD_BU(6, 2, CLKOFF + 14),
211:                                   LD_BU(7, 2, CLKOFF + 13), LD_BU(8, 2, CLKOFF + 12)])
212:                  + ["print r4", "print r6", "print r7", "print r8"])
213: row("devsig yr", printed(t, "r4"), "0x%x" % bcd(g.tm_year % 100),
214:     "0x1fff = BCD(host UTC year%100) -- RAM or a fallback answers 0")
215: row("devsig mon", printed(t, "r6"), "0x%x" % bcd(g.tm_mon),
216:     "0x1ffe = BCD(host UTC month); py tm_mon is ALREADY 1-based")
217: row("devsig mday", printed(t, "r7"), "0x%x" % bcd(g.tm_mday),
218:     "0x1ffd = BCD(host UTC day of month)")
219: row("devsig wday", printed(t, "r8"), "0x%x" % (((g.tm_wday + 1) % 7) + 1),
220:     "0x1ffc = tm_wday+1 RAW, NOT BCD -- a second encoding, same block")
221: 
222: t, how = session(SETUP + program([ST_W(5, 3, 0), LD_W(4, 3, 0), LD_W(4, 3, 0)])
223:                  + ["print r4"])
224: row("shape", "%s/%s" % (how, printed(t, "r4")), "exit0/0x5a5a5a5a",
225:     "the session SHAPE with a RAM load in the access slot does not kill")
226: 
227: # sibling-offset attribution controls
228: for lbl, off, wr in (("ok rd 1fef", 0x1fef, 0), ("ok wr 1fef", 0x1fef, 1),
229:                      ("ok rd 1ff8", 0x1ff8, 0), ("ok wr 1ff8", 0x1ff8, 1),
230:                      ("ok rd 1ff9", 0x1ff9, 0), ("ok wr 1ff9", 0x1ff9, 1)):
231:     w = ST_B(5, 2, off) if wr else LD_BU(4, 2, off)
232:     t, how = access_session(w)
233:     row(lbl, "%s/%s" % (how, printed(t, "r4")), "exit0/0x5a5a5a5a",
234:         "0x%04x %s -- accepted arm, must run to completion"
235:         % (off, "wr" if wr else "rd"))
236: 
237: #  ------------------------------------------------------------- CSR RISING EDGE
238: #
239: #  *** THIS ROW EXISTS BECAUSE THE FIRST KILL TABLE FOUND A FLOOR BREACH: M_ICSR
240: #  ESCAPED 47/47. ***  That mutant moves the accept block's UPPER bound to
241: #  CLKOFF+ICSR, making the condition `addr < 0x1ff8 || addr >= 0x1ff8' a TAUTOLOGY --
242: #  every address is accepted and the switch is NEVER REACHED, so 0x1ff8 is served as
243: #  plain RAM and the control register stops doing its work.
244: #
245: #  The rows `ok rd 1ff8' / `ok wr 1ff8' could not see it, and the reason is the one
246: #  this project keeps re-learning: THEY ARE SURVIVE-ONLY AT THAT OFFSET.  They assert
247: #  the access ran to completion, which is true on the RAM path too.  A survival oracle
248: #  cannot distinguish two arms that both survive.
249: #
250: #  THE CHANNEL THAT DOES VARY is the ICSR case's SIDE EFFECT.  Writing the READ bit
251: #  0 -> 1 calls mk48txx_update_regs(), refreshing the clock registers from the host.
252: #  So: clear ICSR, poison a clock slot, drive the rising edge, read the slot back.
253: #  Fixed build -> the poison is gone, replaced by the host value.  M_ICSR -> the
254: #  poison survives, because no update ever ran.
255: #
256: #  r5's low byte is 0x5a, and 0x5a & MK48TXX_CSR_READ (0x40) is 0x40, so ST_B of r5
257: #  BOTH poisons a slot and drives the edge -- no new instruction encoding is needed.
258: #  0x5a is a safe poison: BCD day-of-month never exceeds 0x31.
259: t, how = session(SETUP + program([ST_B(0, 2, CLKOFF + 8),      # ICSR = 0, READ bit clear
260:                                   ST_B(5, 2, CLKOFF + 13),     # poison the mday slot
261:                                   ST_B(5, 2, CLKOFF + 8),      # READ bit 0 -> 1
262:                                   LD_BU(4, 2, CLKOFF + 13)])
263:                  + ["print r4"])
264: row("csr rise", printed(t, "r4"), "0x%x" % bcd(g.tm_mday),
265:     "ICSR read-bit 0->1 must REFRESH the clock regs (kills M_ICSR)")
266: 
267: # ------------------------------------------------------------------ the kills
268: #  full census: eight offsets x two directions, all byte-width, all guest-reachable
269: for wr in (0, 1):
270:     for off in range(CLKOFF, CLKOFF + ICSR):          # 0x1ff0 .. 0x1ff7
271:         w = ST_B(5, 2, off) if wr else LD_BU(4, 2, off)
272:         t, how = access_session(w)
273:         tag = "%s %04x" % ("wr" if wr else "rd", off)
274:         want_msg = ("[ mk48txx: unimplemented %s offset 0x%x"
275:                     % ("WRITE to" if wr else "READ from", off))
276:         if want_msg in t:
277:             msgs.append(tag)
278:         row(tag, how, "exit0",
279:             "%s of 0x%04x must not end the host process"
280:             % ("st.b" if wr else "ld.bu", off))
281:         row(tag + " obs", printed(t, "r4") if how == "exit0" else "died",
282:             "0x5a5a5a5a",
283:             "...and the guest runs on -- kills a death however it is spelled")
284: 
285: #  INVERTED FROM THE WITNESS, DELIBERATELY, and this is the only row whose
286: #  polarity differs.  The witness asserted 16 -- the defect naming its own
287: #  offsets -- which is green only while the defect is LIVE.  A detector must
288: #  assert the post-fix truth: the eight offsets are ordinary NVRAM and produce
289: #  NO unimplemented-access diagnostic at all.  KILLS: any mutant restoring the
290: #  old boundary brings all sixteen messages back.  CHANNEL: the count itself,
291: #  which varies 0 <-> 16 across that edit, so the row cannot be vacuous.
292: row("msg silent", len(msgs), 0,
293:     "NO access to 0x1ff0..0x1ff7 may announce an unimplemented offset")
294: 
295: bad = [n for n, ok in rows if not ok]
296: print("MK48_RESULT=%d/%d" % (len(rows) - len(bad), len(rows)))
297: print("MK48_LABEL=%s" % LABEL)
298: print("MK48_ELAPSED=%.1fs" % (time.time() - t0))
299: if bad:
300:     print("MK48_RED=%s" % ",".join(bad))
301: print("MK48_WITNESS_DONE")

regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:53:   must keep working.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:54:4. **WHAT BREAKS.** Does any in-tree guest, PROM image or driver depend on the current behaviour? Is
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:55:   there any offset where accepting a write would corrupt something the model later reads back as a clock
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:56:   value? **The clock registers are re-derived from the host on demand -- check whether a guest WRITE to
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:57:   them is silently discarded, silently honoured, or honoured and then overwritten**, because that
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:58:   determines whether `d->reg[]` is a safe backing store or a shared one.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:59:5. **THE DETECTOR.** If this round ships a fix, what should its rows be? The vacuity floor requires the
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:60:   detector to fail on the pre-fix build, on a verbatim reinstatement of the removed kill, on a null
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:61:   medium, AND on a NULLED REPAIR (the guard kept, its effect removed). Name the rows and, for each, the
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:62:   mutant it kills and the observable that differs under that mutant. **A row whose named channel cannot
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:63:   vary under its named mutant is vacuous by construction, and that is knowable now, before anything
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:64:   runs.**
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:65:6. **WHAT ELSE IS FATAL OR UNREACHABLE IN THIS FILE?** While you are in it: any other offset, any other
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:66:   `exit(`, any decode that cannot be reached.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:67:7. If you found something else, say so.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:68:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/brief_mk48_design.md:69:Read-only: no builds, no probe runs, no edits, no patches. Prose and tables only.

regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:9:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:10:The two bounds of the accept block are the siblings, and they are ASYMMETRIC ON PURPOSE:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:11:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:12:    lower  CLKOFF + ICSR   (0x1ff8)   -- CHANGED by this diff
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:13:    upper  CLKOFF + ISEC   (0x1ff9)   -- UNCHANGED
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:14:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:15:After the change the block admits everything except the single offset 0x1ff8, which is
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:16:exactly the one case the switch implements.  The asymmetry is therefore not an oversight:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:17:the window has collapsed to the width of the implemented case.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:18:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:19:  ROW OWED, and it is the one this check exists to find: the upper bound is now load-
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:20:  bearing in a way it was not before.  Before the diff, moving it would have shifted a
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:21:  nine-offset window; after it, moving it by one either swallows the implemented case
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:22:  (ISEC -> ICSR, window empty) or re-admits a clock register (ISEC -> ISEC+1).  Rows
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:23:  `ok rd 1ff8' / `ok wr 1ff8' and `ok rd 1ff9' / `ok wr 1ff9' already exist and pin both
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:24:  sides.  NO NEW ROW OWED -- discharged by rows the witness already carried.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:25:

regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:159:    rather than resolved.  MEASURED 30/47.  The arithmetic that got there was right and
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:160:    the first number was not; recording both is the point of writing predictions down.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:161:  * M_ICSR was predicted 43/47 on the reasoning that the `ok rd/wr 1ff8' pair would
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:162:    redden.  **MEASURED 47/47.  The prediction was WRONG and the reason is instructive.**
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:163:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:164:WHY M_ICSR ESCAPED, derived rather than guessed:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:165:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:166:    FIXED:   accept if  addr < 0x1ff8 || addr >= 0x1ff9   -> switch sees ONLY 0x1ff8
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:167:    M_ICSR:  accept if  addr < 0x1ff8 || addr >= 0x1ff8   -> A TAUTOLOGY
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:168:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:169:  Every address is accepted, the switch is NEVER REACHED, and 0x1ff8 is served as plain
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:170:  RAM.  The control register silently stops doing its work.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:171:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:172:  *** AND THE ROWS AIMED AT THAT OFFSET COULD NOT SEE IT, BY CONSTRUCTION. ***  `ok rd
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:173:  1ff8' and `ok wr 1ff8' assert only that the access RAN TO COMPLETION -- which is true on
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:174:  the RAM path too.  They are SURVIVE-ONLY at that offset, which is this project's own
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:175:  named vacuity: a survival oracle cannot separate two arms that both survive.  The
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:176:  CHANNEL block above named their channel as "the implemented case stops being reached;
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:177:  exit/obs change", and that was simply false -- neither exit status nor the scratch
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:178:  observable moves.  **A channel asserted at writing time and never checked is exactly
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:179:  what the CHANNEL rule exists to catch, and here the rule was applied and got the answer
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:180:  wrong.**  What would have caught it is asking not "can this row vary?" but "can it vary
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:181:  UNDER THIS MUTANT?" -- the mutant-specific form.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:182:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:183:THE REPAIR, in-round under L2: ONE ROW, `csr rise', reading the ICSR case's SIDE EFFECT.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:184:Writing the READ bit 0 -> 1 calls mk48txx_update_regs(), refreshing the clock registers
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:185:from the host.  So the row clears ICSR, POISONS a clock slot, drives the rising edge, and
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:186:reads the slot back:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:187:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:188:    fixed build   -> the poison is gone, replaced by the host value   (0x10 today)
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:189:    M_ICSR        -> the poison SURVIVES, because no update ever ran  (0x5a)
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:190:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:191:  It needs no new instruction encoding: r5's low byte is 0x5a, and 0x5a & MK48TXX_CSR_READ
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:192:  (0x40) is 0x40, so one ST_B of r5 both poisons a slot and drives the edge.  0x5a is a
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:193:  safe poison because a BCD day-of-month never exceeds 0x31.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:194:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:195:  MEASURED GREEN on the fixed build before table 2: the detector is 48/48.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:196:
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:197:TABLE 2 RE-RUNS EVERY LEG, not just M_ICSR, because this project has measured that added
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:198:rows perturb other mutants' scores.  Table 1 is preserved at t1/ rather than overwritten.
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:199:

regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:222:discovered later.  *This project has measured that added rows change other mutants'
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:223:scores; here the change is exactly the new row's own denominator, and the re-table is what
regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:224:establishes that rather than assuming it.*
