1275: ## THE VACUITY FLOOR AND THE DE-ESCALATION CLAUSE (flagship adjudication, 2026-08-21)
1276: 
1277: **"Weak earns its own round; vacuous does not get to ship" IS A RATIONALISATION UNTIL A FLOOR IS
1278: ATTACHED** �?" the adjudicating seat said so about the main loop's own call, and it was right:
1279: *weak* has no lower bound, so any detector that kills one mutant can claim it.
1280: 
1281: > **THE FLOOR.** A detector is VACUOUS �?" replace it IN-ROUND �?" unless it (a) fails on the
1282: > pre-fix build, (b) fails on a **VERBATIM REINSTATEMENT of the removed defect at EVERY fixed
1283: > site**, and (c) fails on a null medium (a stale copy, a comment-only file �?" i.e. it carries
1284: > identity rows). Below the floor, the round's own claim to have "shipped a detector with the
1285: > fix" is a WRONG RECORD, which the stopping rule already fixes in-round.
1286: >
1287: > ### *** CLAUSE (d) �?" NULL-REPAIR RED. Adopted 2026-08-23 by OWNER RULING, on the round
1288: > that measured its case. ***
1289: >
1290: > **(d) The detector must also fail when the fix is PRESENT but its REPAIR IS NULLED �?"
1291: > the guard kept, its effect removed.**
1292: >
1293: > **(b) DOES NOT IMPLY (d), and #461 measured both in the same hour on the same detector:**
1294: >
1295: > | mutant | shape | score |
1296: > |---|---|---|
1297: > | `m_dmarevert` | (b): reinstate the `exit(1)` verbatim | **22/31 �?" killed** |
1298: > | `m_align1` | (d): keep the guard, weaken `& 3` to `& 1` | **27/27 �?" ESCAPED** |
1299: >
1300: > The detector passed (a), (b) and (c) and was still blind to a ONE-CHARACTER edit that
1301: > re-opened the very defect the round had just closed. (b) restores the DEFECT; (d) neuters
1302: > the FIX. A detector can be sensitive to one and blind to the other, because they fail at
1303: > different sites: (b) fails where the old code was, (d) fails where the new code is.
1304: >
1305: > **HOW TO BUILD THE (d) MUTANT �?" and it is the same discriminator the accept-row rule
1306: > already uses.** Take each predicate the fix **DEPENDS ON** and weaken it to the
1307: > least-restrictive form that still *reads* like the same check: a mask narrowed
1308: > (`& 3` -> `& 1`), a condition qualified (`if (X)` -> `if (X && writeflag == MEM_WRITE)`),
1309: > a bound relaxed. **Not deletion** �?" deletion is (b) wearing (d)'s clothes and is usually
1310: > already covered. The test is whether a plausible future edit could produce it.
1311: >
1312: > **WHY THE ONE-CHARACTER FORM IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS.** `dma alias`, the row written
1313: > specifically to pin that repair, was **STRUCTURALLY BLIND** to it: it used an ODD address,
1314: > which both guard forms drop. *The row aimed at the defect could not see the defect return.*
1315: > A row written from the FIX's intent tests the intent, not the boundary �?" so (d)'s mutant
1316: > has to come from an adversary, which is exactly what the ask-again buys.
1317: >
1318: > **COST: one mutant per repair predicate**, the same order as the accept-row rule (#448 = 4,
1319: > #449 = 1, #461 = 4 across two arms). It is bounded by the diff, so it cannot become #392:
1320: > the predicates are the ones the fix ADDED, and that list is finite and visible.
1321: >
1322: > ### *** "THE PREDICATE THE FIX INTRODUCED" ENUMERATED THE EMPTY SET FOR MOST OF THIS
1323: > ### QUEUE, AND NOBODY NOTICED FOR THREE ROUNDS. Corrected 2026-08-24 -- clause (d) now
1324: > ### reads DEPENDS ON, not INTRODUCED. ***
1325: >
1326: > Clause (d) says *keep the guard, null its repair*. As written it enumerated only
1327: > predicates the diff ADDS -- and **the two commonest fix shapes here add none**:
1328: >
1329: > * a **pure deletion** introduces no predicate at all;
1330: > * a **DE-FATALISATION** introduces none either -- it changes what an EXISTING
1331: >   predicate's branch does. That is most of this project's queue.
1332: >
1333: > So for those rounds clause (d) enumerated **nothing**, and a round could satisfy the
1334: > floor while having built no (d) mutant whatever. `#461` measured (d) successfully only
1335: > because its fix happened to ADD a predicate (an alignment mask) for the mutant to weaken.
1336: >
1337: > **Reading it as DEPENDS ON fixes both cases at once**, and it is why this now replaces a
1338: > narrower deletion-only rule written earlier the same day: that rule was a special case of
1339: > this gap, and two overlapping statements of one rule is the accumulation this carrier is
1340: > supposed to refuse.
1341: >
1342: > **The two shapes, once the enumeration is right:**
1343: > * **fix ADDS a predicate** -> weaken the added one (unchanged; the original rule).
1344: > * **fix adds none** -> the (d) mutant reroutes an EXISTING guard's repair. For a
1345: >   de-fatalisation: **swap the guard's operand for a field the guarded BODY reads.**
1346: >   For a pure deletion: **re-introduce the removed behaviour under a DIFFERENT predicate**
1347: >   -- not the same one widened, which is (b) wearing (d)'s clothes.
1348: >
1349: > *** THE MEASURED CASE, and it is why this matters rather than being tidy: ***
1350: > `#462` de-fatalised two arms. Its guard reads `d->data`; the body it guards reads
1351: > `d->data` AND `d->data_len`; the two are set together in one place and cleared in two
1352: > places that touch only ONE of them. Swapping the guard's operand to `d->data_len == 0`
1353: > **scored a CLEAN SWEEP on a 52-row detector that had just passed twelve mutants**, and
1354: > restored a NULL-pointer read of 408 bytes. Under the old wording that was coverage debt;
1355: > under DEPENDS ON it is a **floor breach, L2, fix in-round** -- which is what the round
1356: > did, before the wording was corrected.
1357: >
1358: > #### *** THE BOUND, and it is not optional: "DEPENDS ON" INVITES TRANSITIVE DATA-FLOW
1359: > #### CHASING, which is unbounded and is how this becomes `#392`. Named 2026-08-24 by the
1360: > #### adjudicating seat, in the same breath as upholding the correction. ***
1361: >
1362: > > **DEPENDS ON enumerates exactly two things: (1) the predicates DOMINATING the diff's
1363: > > edited lines, walked up only to the ENCLOSING FUNCTION BOUNDARY -- never into callees
1364: > > or callers; and (2) for operand-swap forms, swaps drawn ONLY from fields the guarded
1365: > > BODY reads (the guard-field/body-field grep the pre-table check already mandates).**
1366: >
1367: > That is finite, derivable from the diff by reading, and **it provably contains the
1368: > measured case**: the `d->data == NULL` guard dominates the edited decline (clause 1), and
1369: > `data_len` is a body-read field (clause 2), so `M11` falls out mechanically rather than
1370: > by inspiration. It cannot become `#392` because the enumeration stays closed over the
1371: > diff's own hunks -- the same closure the INTRODUCED wording had, without the empty-set
1372: > defect that made INTRODUCED enumerate nothing for a de-fatalisation.
1373: >
1374: > #### *** JOINT CELLS -- adjudicated 2026-09-04 on `pcc2widthkills` pass 10 by the flagship seat, after
1375: > #### a measuring seat's CONJUNCTION of two body-read counts escaped 190/190 AND THE SAME SEAT ARGUED IT
1376: > #### WAS NOT A BREACH.  Both proposed bounds were rejected; the one adopted came from neither. ***
1377: >
1378: > > **DEPENDS-ON enumerates JOINT cells over the guard's operands and the body-read lvalues EXACTLY
1379: > > WHEN THE GUARDED BODY'S OWN CODE DISTINGUISHES THEM.**  For each lvalue take the PARTITION the
1380: > > body's own tests induce (`pending > 0` gives {0, >0}; `relative_addr == PCCTWO_T1ICR` gives
1381: > > {T1, T2}); the enumerated cells are the CROSS-PRODUCT of those partitions -- never arbitrary
1382: > > boolean functions of the read-set.  A mutant exempting a single cell whose reachability is
1383: > > MEASURED is a floor breach; close the whole cross-product in ONE diff and write into the
1384: > > contract which cells are rowed.  A value or combination the body never tests as a case
1385: > > (`idata == k`; a byte no conditional distinguishes; both counts >= 2 where the body tests `> 0`)
1386: > > is DISCLOSED under clause (e).
1387: >
1388: > **Why this form and not the two that were argued.**  The dissent's discriminator -- *a plausible
1389: > future edit* -- failed three ways: plausibility is not derivable by reading; the mutant was written
1390: > and measured, so it is an edit someone writes; and "single-operand is complete" is measurably
1391: > false, since `&& idata != 2` is single-operand and escapes.  The main loop's own bound -- *pairs of
1392: > body-read lvalues over a finite reachable space* -- was rejected as SELF-REFUTING: the counts' raw
1393: > value space is 2^32 apiece, so "finite" does no bounding work on its motivating case (a C `int` is
1394: > finite -- the first wording here said it was not; codex and glm caught it on pass 11); "reachable
1395: > guest state" would force rowing
1396: > `idata == 2` that the same record discloses; and a PAIRS cap misses `#499`'s four axes.  The
1397: > body-tested-partition form is finite (a product of small partition counts), derivable from the
1398: > diff by reading the body's own `if`s, and it enumerates `#499`'s width x component x direction x
1399: > position cells from `len`, `writeflag` and `relative_addr` -- which neither argued bound reached.
1400: >
1401: > **The measured case.**  The TxICR body tests both pending counts at `> 0` and the label at
1402: > `== T1ICR` / `T2ICR`, so the closure is {T2, T1} x {p1 = 0, > 0} x {p2 = 0, > 0} -- eight cells.
1403: > Pass 9 held one open cell (both counts, T2 label: a 190/190 escape, predicted by the Opus seat
1404: > before its build); pass 10 found the MIRROR (both counts, T1 label) and the (p2 only, T1 label)
1405: > cell open -- the `#499` shape reproduced inside the repair for it -- and the label x p1 x p2 x IEN
1406: > product (sixteen cells) closed in one diff of nine readbacks in existing sessions; the ICLR, INT and
1407: > 0xe0 partitions were disclosed by count, and pass 11 then found the ICLR x pending cell open and
1408: > in-rule (this paragraph said "eight cells ... three readbacks" until pass 11).  **The De Morgan form of the gap**, from the kimi
1409: > substitute: `!(p1 && p2)` is `p1 == 0 || p2 == 0`; neither disjunct is true of all its lvalue's
1410: > held values, the disjunction is true in every row, so a per-lvalue disclosure test can never fire
1411: > on it.  That is why the per-lvalue value-domain statement, correct as far as it went, could not
1412: > see this class at all.
1413: >
1414: > **Two corollaries from the same pass, both measured.**  The PRE-TABLE CHECK's SIBLING PARITY row
1415: > compares BLOCKS; at a block that serves TWO case labels the siblings are the LABELS, and a
1416: > parity census over the row names is what would have shown `pendboth` as the only T2-only cell
1417: > before its table ran (Opus, pass 10).  **AND THE JOIN ITSELF, adjudicated 2026-09-09 on `sgimtedepth4dead` 7g-1 after a MEASURED total escape: a dispatch LABEL is a floor axis of an edited guard when the selector's value FLOWS INTO an lvalue the guarded body reads or writes within the enclosing function (there, `dst_mode = (dst_tlb << 10) + ...` two lines above a repair of `dst_mode |= RGBA`), or when the body re-tests it after the join (TxICR); a label that reaches the edit and touches nothing the body reads is clause (e) (the `mode & COPY` shape).  A detector rowing one label of three was swept 14/14 by `&& dst_tlb == MTE_TLB_A` while a guest-only control showed the closed defect live at the other two (`regress/artifacts/sgimtedepth4dead/killtable3_20260909/`).  The floor it adds is {labels} x the guard operand's body-tested partition -- six cells there, two rows owed -- never the labels' cross-product with everything.**  And the measuring seat's own bound -- the cross-product
1418: > of the value-CLASSES the rows establish, with the dispatch axes as full axes, demand
1419: > INCREMENTAL -- agrees with the body-tested form on every cell that mattered here and differs on
1420: > where the classes come from (the rows, or the body's tests); that residual difference is
1421: > carried to the next pass as a named question rather than settled by the author.
1422: >
1423: > #### *** THE LETTER OVER-DEMANDED, AND ITS OWN AUTHOR BOUNDED IT ONE PASS LATER (pass 11, 2026-09-04). ***
1424: >
1425: > Read literally, "the cross-product of the body-tested partitions" demanded ALL 128 cells at the TxICR
1426: > arm -- the body also tests `idata & IEN`, `idata & ICLR`, the register's INT bit and `idata & 0xe0` --
1427: > and it compounds as 2^k in the `if`-count across twelve arms: the `#392` shape wearing the rule's
1428: > clothes.  Three seats said the letter demands the remainder; the flagship seat, asked to be harder on
1429: > its own rule than on anyone, ruled AGAINST the letter:
1430: >
1431: > > **The rule ENUMERATES joint cells wherever the body distinguishes them; the FLOOR -- the cells a row
1432: > > is OWED for -- is the cross-product of the guard's dominating predicates with the device-STATE lvalues
1433: > > the fix's own repair path reads that are JOINTLY REACHABLE through its own data flow.**  The bits of
1434: > > the single value being written are single-operand MARGINALS, not floor; joint cells outside the floor
1435: > > are clause-(e) coverage debt, subject to the one ask-again.  A filter may reject or discharge a cell
1436: > > but may demand a new row only for a floor cell -- so it cannot grow unbounded.
1437: >
1438: > At TxICR the floor is {label} x {p1} x {p2}: one `pcc_timer_tick` callback increments both counts and
1439: > `reassert_interrupts` reads both, so the pair is a joint input of the model.  Eight cells, all rowed.
1440: >
1441: > #### *** THE FLOOR INCLUDES THE SINGLE-AXIS GENERATING SET, AND "DERIVED" STATE IS NOT DISPOSABLE.
1442: > #### Adjudicated 2026-09-04 by the fix review (Fable 5.1 adjudicating; GPT-6 Astra at max dissenting on the
1443: > #### bound and supplying the two findings that made the amendment measurable). ***
1444: >
1445: > > **At each floor cell, every idata bit the guarded body TESTS is written both clear and set.**  The bare
1446: > > state floor above does not catch `M_ICLR` -- an idata-bit x state joint -- and would grade the pass-11
1447: > > escape as debt while the CHANGELOG grades it L2; the generating set is what makes the two consistent.
1448: > > Multi-axis idata joints stay clause-(e) debt.  **The codex seat adopts the Opus strength-t covering
1449: > > array instead** (its `t` = the body's longest conjunction) and is RECORDED, not adopted -- for COST (seven

1451: > > because a covering array certifies that a tuple OCCURS, not that an observable differs, so it cannot be the
1452: > > floor without the CHANNEL obligation doing the work.  *This clause first said "`t` is a judgment input";
1453: > > the array's own author refuted that on pass 13 -- `t` is read off the body -- and supplied the two grounds.*
1454: >

1464: >   the enumeration did not know it had.  *An enumeration is checked against the BODY and against the ROWS

1469: >   it, the body clears it only on ICLR, and an IEN-clear byte write zeroes the count -- so (INT set, count 0)
1470: >   is a reachable joint state of two body-read lvalues that no row held.  **Reachability of a joint cell is

157: TWO PREDICTIONS TO SCORE HONESTLY:
158:   * M_HALF was predicted at "39/47 ... no, 30/47", written with the uncertainty stated
159:     rather than resolved.  MEASURED 30/47.  The arithmetic that got there was right and
160:     the first number was not; recording both is the point of writing predictions down.
161:   * M_ICSR was predicted 43/47 on the reasoning that the `ok rd/wr 1ff8' pair would
162:     redden.  **MEASURED 47/47.  The prediction was WRONG and the reason is instructive.**
163: 
164: WHY M_ICSR ESCAPED, derived rather than guessed:
165: 
166:     FIXED:   accept if  addr < 0x1ff8 || addr >= 0x1ff9   -> switch sees ONLY 0x1ff8
167:     M_ICSR:  accept if  addr < 0x1ff8 || addr >= 0x1ff8   -> A TAUTOLOGY
168: 
169:   Every address is accepted, the switch is NEVER REACHED, and 0x1ff8 is served as plain
170:   RAM.  The control register silently stops doing its work.
171: 
172:   *** AND THE ROWS AIMED AT THAT OFFSET COULD NOT SEE IT, BY CONSTRUCTION. ***  `ok rd
173:   1ff8' and `ok wr 1ff8' assert only that the access RAN TO COMPLETION -- which is true on
174:   the RAM path too.  They are SURVIVE-ONLY at that offset, which is this project's own
175:   named vacuity: a survival oracle cannot separate two arms that both survive.  The
176:   CHANNEL block above named their channel as "the implemented case stops being reached;
177:   exit/obs change", and that was simply false -- neither exit status nor the scratch
178:   observable moves.  **A channel asserted at writing time and never checked is exactly
179:   what the CHANNEL rule exists to catch, and here the rule was applied and got the answer
180:   wrong.**  What would have caught it is asking not "can this row vary?" but "can it vary
181:   UNDER THIS MUTANT?" -- the mutant-specific form.
182: 
183: THE REPAIR, in-round under L2: ONE ROW, `csr rise', reading the ICSR case's SIDE EFFECT.
184: Writing the READ bit 0 -> 1 calls mk48txx_update_regs(), refreshing the clock registers
185: from the host.  So the row clears ICSR, POISONS a clock slot, drives the rising edge, and
186: reads the slot back:
187: 
188:     fixed build   -> the poison is gone, replaced by the host value   (0x10 today)
189:     M_ICSR        -> the poison SURVIVES, because no update ever ran  (0x5a)
190: 
191:   It needs no new instruction encoding: r5's low byte is 0x5a, and 0x5a & MK48TXX_CSR_READ
192:   (0x40) is 0x40, so one ST_B of r5 both poisons a slot and drives the edge.  0x5a is a
193:   safe poison because a BCD day-of-month never exceeds 0x31.
194: 
195:   MEASURED GREEN on the fixed build before table 2: the detector is 48/48.
196: 
197: TABLE 2 RE-RUNS EVERY LEG, not just M_ICSR, because this project has measured that added
198: rows perturb other mutants' scores.  Table 1 is preserved at t1/ rather than overwritten.
199: 
200: --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
201: TABLE 2 (t2/SCORES.txt), the repaired 48-row instrument.  ZERO ESCAPES.
202: 
203:   BASE      48/48
204:   M_OLD     15/48   32 kill rows + `msg silent'
205:   M_HALF    31/48   the four re-admitted offsets, both directions, both rows + `msg silent'
206:   M_ICSR    47/48   *** KILLED, by `csr rise' AND BY NOTHING ELSE ***
207:   M_NULL    22/48   live, nvram0, four devsig, the accept pair, `csr rise', 16 read rows,
208:                     `msg silent'
209:   HEAD      15/48   whole-file null medium
210:   RESTORED  48/48   cmp-vs-SEC CLEAN, binary FRESH, sentinel cleared on both parts
211: 
212: *** THE NEW ROW IS NECESSARY AND NON-REDUNDANT, which is the thing worth checking about
213: any row added to close a breach. ***  M_ICSR scores 47/48 with `csr rise' as its ONLY red:
214: no other row in the instrument sees that mutant, so the row is not decoration and the
215: breach was real.  Conversely `csr rise' is green under M_OLD, M_HALF and HEAD -- those
216: leave 0x1ff8 reaching the switch -- so it is aimed rather than indiscriminate.
217: 
218: AND THE ADDED ROW DID NOT PERTURB THE OTHER LEGS' KILL SETS.  Every other score rose by
219: EXACTLY ONE (14->15, 30->31, 47->48) and every FAILING list is unchanged except for
220: M_NULL, which gains `csr rise' because removing the backing store also removes the poison
221: write the row depends on.  That is the expected coupling and it is stated rather than
222: discovered later.  *This project has measured that added rows change other mutants'
223: scores; here the change is exactly the new row's own denominator, and the re-table is what
224: establishes that rather than assuming it.*
225: 
226: THE FLOOR, now satisfied and each clause pointed at its leg:
227:   (a) fails pre-fix                        -> HEAD 15/48
228:   (b) restated: restores the OLD DISPATCH  -> M_OLD 15/48   (the literal clause is
229:                                               unsatisfiable here, as recorded above)
230:   (c) null medium                          -> HEAD 15/48
231:   (d) NULLED REPAIR                        -> M_NULL 22/48


    Directory: C:\DocumentNoSnc\CC\GXEMUL\GXEMUL-SEC\regress\artifacts\mk48nvramkill\killtable_20260910

-a----         9/10/2026   1:46 PM           3697 mutate.py                                                            
#!/usr/bin/env python3
#  #521 mk48nvramkill mutator.  One named literal find/replace per mutant.
#  MUTANT-TEXT-NOT-FOUND is exit 9, a HARD FAILURE -- so every mutant doubles as a
#  PRESENCE PIN on the fix's own text: if the fix is edited away, the table cannot
#  silently score a build that no longer contains it.
#  Usage: mutate.py <path-to-clone-dev_mk48txx.c> <mutant>
import io, sys

P, m = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
T = '\t'

FIXED_LOW = T + "if (relative_addr < MK48T08_CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ICSR ||\n"
UPPER     = T + "    relative_addr >= MK48T08_CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ISEC) {\n"
#  THE STORE LINE APPEARS TWICE -- once in the accept block and once in the ICSR case
#  arm -- so the accept-block one is anchored by the `goto ret;' that follows it.  The
#  exactly-one-match rule CAUGHT this before the table ran, which is the presence pin
#  doing its job rather than a near miss.
STORE     = T*3 + "d->reg[relative_addr] = idata;\n" + T*2 + "goto ret;\n"

M = {
    #  BASE: the fix, unmutated.  Present so the base score comes from the same driver
    #  and the same frozen probe as every other leg.
    'BASE': None,

    #  M_OLD -- RESTORE THE OLD DISPATCH.  This is the round's stand-in for floor clause
    #  (b): the diff removes no kill statement, it makes one UNREACHABLE, so a verbatim
    #  reinstatement of the `default:' arm is a no-op or dead code and no channel can see
    #  it.  Putting the LOWER BOUND back is the distinguishable form, and it restores the
    #  defect exactly.
    'M_OLD': (FIXED_LOW, T + "if (relative_addr < MK48T08_CLKOFF ||\n"),

    #  M_HALF -- a PARTIAL boundary move.  The repair's own predicate, weakened to the
    #  least-restrictive form that still reads like the same check.  Offsets 0x1ff4..7
    #  remain fatal.  A detector that passes this has pinned the bound's existence and
    #  not its VALUE.
    'M_HALF': (FIXED_LOW, T + "if (relative_addr < MK48T08_CLKOFF + 4 ||\n"),

    #  M_ICSR -- move the UPPER bound instead, so the window is empty and the implemented
    #  control case is never reached.  The near/far-bound lesson: a bound is pinned only
    #  by a row that sits on it.
    'M_ICSR': (UPPER, T + "    relative_addr >= MK48T08_CLKOFF + MK48TXX_ICSR) {\n"),

    #  M_NULL -- THE NULLED REPAIR, floor clause (d).  The corrected guard is KEPT and its
    #  effect removed: accesses still take the accept path, but a write stores nothing.
    #  A survival oracle cannot see this -- both forms survive -- which is why the round
    #  needs a round-trip row rather than a "the host is still alive" row.
    'M_NULL': (STORE, T*3 + "/*  M_NULL: backing store removed  */\n" + T*2 + "goto ret;\n"),

    #  HEAD -- whole-file null medium: the committed pre-fix source.
    'HEAD': ('WHOLE-FILE', None),
}

if m not in M:
    print('unknown mutant', m); sys.exit(9)

spec = M[m]
if spec is None:
    print('MUTATE BASE: baseline installed unmutated'); sys.exit(0)

if spec[0] == 'WHOLE-FILE':
    import subprocess
    r = subprocess.run(['git', '-C', '/mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC',
                        'show', 'HEAD:src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c'],
                       stdout=subprocess.PIPE)
    if r.returncode != 0 or not r.stdout:
        print('HEAD checkout FAILED'); sys.exit(9)
    io.open(P, 'wb').write(r.stdout)
    print('MUTATE HEAD: committed pre-fix file installed (whole-file null medium)')
    sys.exit(0)

old, new = spec
s = io.open(P, encoding='utf-8', newline='').read()
c = s.count(old)
if c != 1:
    print('MUTANT-TEXT-NOT-FOUND: %s matched %d times' % (m, c)); sys.exit(9)
io.open(P, 'w', encoding='utf-8', newline='').write(s.replace(old, new))
print('MUTATE %s: applied' % m)

18055-the `uint16_t`, so every ACCEPTED pointer overflows and every non-overflowing one is REJECTED. The three
18056-arrays are written by ordinary guest register writes, so the guest picks the value. `re_linear_a` is
18057-`uint32_t` and is NOT affected (unsigned wrap, defined). Fix: three casts to `uint32_t`. Found by the

web search: https://www.st.com/resource/en/datasheet/m48t18.pdf
codex
I found a concrete error in `M_NULL`: replacing the store with only a comment leaves `goto ret` as the body of `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)`. Reads then fall through to the fatal switch. The saved results show exactly that—all write-survival rows stay green, while reads die. The reported `22/48` therefore does not measure the isolated “writes store nothing” mutation described in the brief.

The floor’s actual wording also resolves clause (b): it requires restoring the removed defect, not specifically an `exit()` statement. VERDICT: NOT-RATIFY
PASS2_MK48_20260910

**The product fix is correct; the round’s mutation record needs correction.** I opened the specified tree, verified the actual diff, and confirmed that the current source and detector match their table-2 frozen copies. No builds, probe runs, or edits.

The concrete finding is **a malformed `M_NULL`**. Its [replacement text](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/mutate.py:47) removes the assignment but leaves its unbraced `if`. Comments are whitespace in C, so `goto ret;` becomes the statement controlled by `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)`. Reads consequently fall through to the switch.

The [saved results](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/t2/probe_M_NULL.raw:4) confirm this: all eight target read sessions die, all write-survival rows pass, and eight diagnostics appear. `live` returns **absent**, not the zero expected from a surviving read after a suppressed write. Thus `22/48` does not establish the isolated store-null behavior described in the record. This is an evidence defect, **not a demonstrated second floor escape**.

1. **The lower bound is right.** `CLKOFF + ICSR = 0x1ff8`, also `8192 − 8`. The [corrected condition](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_mk48txx.c:116) leaves exactly `{0x1ff8}` reaching the switch. Its case and READ-bit side effect remain intact.

   The hardware premise also holds: ST’s datasheet places the eight clock/control bytes at `0x1ff8..0x1fff`, leaving `0x1ff0..0x1ff7` in ordinary SRAM. [Manufacturer datasheet, pp. 7–8](https://www.st.com/resource/en/datasheet/m48t18.pdf)

2. **The upper bound is right for this dispatch.** Keeping `ISEC = 9` routes the seven time registers through their backing array while preserving special handling of ICSR. Moving it downward swallows ICSR; moving it upward exposes seconds to the fatal default.

   That establishes correct routing, not complete RTC emulation. The implementation refreshes at initialization and READ-bit rising edges; continuous updates and WRITE-bit clock-setting behavior remain separate, existing limitations. The hardware specifies those additional behaviors. [Manufacturer datasheet, p. 14](https://www.st.com/resource/en/datasheet/m48t18.pdf)

3. **`M_OLD` legitimately satisfies clause (b), without an exception.** The [actual rule](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/CLAUDE.md:1281) says “removed defect,” not “removed kill statement.” Here the removed defect is the erroneous lower-bound expression. `M_OLD` restores that executable line verbatim, with its original downstream behavior already present.

   The premise that literal compliance is impossible is therefore mistaken. Reinstating an unchanged `default:` arm would simply be choosing the wrong mutation.

4. **I predict no non-equivalent escape within the documented dispatch-boundary/direction class.** The instrument observes all eight formerly fatal offsets in both directions, the adjacent accepted offsets, and the ICSR refresh effect. Those observations distinguish partial acceptance, renewed rejection, and swallowing the control case.

   A concrete escape **outside that class** is changing the accept-block store’s RHS from `idata` to `idata & 0x7f`. **Prediction: 48/48; no row reddens.** Every device write driven into that block uses `0x5a`, whose high bit is already clear. Arbitrary NVRAM bytes would nevertheless be corrupted.

   This is precisely the unpartitioned `idata` value-space debt disclosed in [PRETABLE](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/mk48nvramkill/killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out:60). Address-aliasing changes, newly introduced width predicates, and broader CSR/RTC semantics also exceed the narrowly established coverage. The 32 survival rows do not establish storage correctness at the eight repaired offsets.

5. **Other channel assignments are wrong, although the rows retain useful purposes.** Against the *intended isolated store-null mutation*, `nvram0` cannot vary: its neighbor was never written. The four `devsig` values remain populated by initialization. The write-side `obs` rows inspect scratch RAM, so they cannot detect lost NVRAM writes either.

   Against the *actual malformed* `M_NULL`, several of these rows do vary because reads die. Calling those rows globally vacuous would be inaccurate; crediting them with detecting suppressed storage is equally inaccurate. In particular, the added `csr rise` failure under `M_NULL` comes from its final read falling through—not merely from failure to poison the clock slot.

6. **Leaving the width defect separate is appropriate.** This change removes process death without changing the existing transfer implementation. It does not establish correct multibyte NVRAM access, and the scope statement should preserve that distinction.

   Also qualify “width-independent” as a property of accesses **delivered to this handler**. The [m88k alignment check](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_m88k_instr_loadstore.c:167) can reject unaligned wider instructions before device dispatch. The byte-access census remains valid.

7. **Two smaller detector claims need tightening.** The [header](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/mk48_nvram_probe.py:4) still advertises the old `47/47` and `14/47` scores. Also, weekday values `1..7` have identical binary and BCD representations: that row checks weekday conversion, not a distinguishable second encoding. Finally, sampling the expected date before several sessions permits false failures across UTC midnight.

The missing measurement to name is **`M_STORE_ONLY`**: suppress the assignment with an explicit empty statement or body, preserving unconditional `goto ret`. **Static prediction: 47/48, solely `live` failing with `0x0`, assuming a stable UTC date.** That measurement would settle the intended storage-channel claim without adding a detector row.
